# Universal-law corpus facts

Updated: 2026-09-11

Scope: compressed facts and logically forced design constraints extracted from audited positive theorems, negative no-gos, frontier branches, and RH endpoints. No active conjectures or work log.

## RH: route-independent research constraints

- **Must check whether a proposed bound also forces simplicity of critical-line zeros.** RH locates zeros; a prescribed rate of growth of an energy bound near the critical line can additionally exclude multiple zeros. Cannot add that rate to a criterion allowing separate constants at every exponent without proving it. The [diagonal-budget strength audit](../route-e-literal-renewal-energy-attack.md#attempt-5-audit-the-strength-of-collective-diagonal-dominance) gives a paper proof of this additional simplicity conclusion; that new implication is not yet formalized in Lean.

- **Cannot infer a nonnegative source–remainder pairing from exact reconstruction or a positive energy kernel.** Must establish the sign for the original coefficients: the complete pairing can remain strictly negative at every sufficiently large cutoff throughout an open parameter interval. Must retain this signed contribution and prove its quantitative compensation before inferring a uniform energy bound. The [literal renewal source audit](../route-e-literal-renewal-energy-attack.md) proves an explicit shifted-moment gap and this persistent negative pairing in Lean; it supplies no upper energy bound.

- **Must distinguish failure at early source depths from exclusion of the original spectral parameter.** A cutoff chosen before integration can admit every later depth at the unchanged parameter. Must prove summability of integrated norms before exchanging the complete source sum and integral, retaining all mixed terms and the original measure. Such admission still supplies no equality with a separately fixed physical observable. The [complete source-admission and reader audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-complete-integrand-admission.md) proves this extension at every original Xi divisor, while preserving a nonzero top-jet response.

- **Cannot gain a power of the cutoff merely by changing between source representations that bound each other uniformly in that cutoff.** Must prove an independent cancellation estimate in at least one representation. Constants depending on a hypothetical zero must remain explicit, particularly when they diverge toward the critical line. The [inverse energy comparison](../edq-full-covariance-core-attack.md#direct-current-phase-cancellation-and-inverse-abel-attack-lean) proves this constraint for the complete arithmetic primitive and chronological current at every hypothetical off-line zero; it does not exclude a new arithmetic argument using the zero equation.

- **Must control constants and starting cutoffs uniformly in order before optimizing an asymptotic estimate.** Separate bounds at every fixed logarithmic order do not justify choosing an order that grows with the cutoff. Even a simultaneous bound with constants growing like `A^q (3q)!` permits a majorant that grows almost linearly; must establish the stronger quantitative order control needed at the original scale. The [complete-source bound and optimization audit](../edq-full-covariance-core-attack.md#complete-source-stretched-exponential-ceiling-lean) supply one common cutoff and explicit factorial constants, and identify the limit of that estimate.

- **Must account for subleading powers and logarithmic terms in original cutoff identities before comparing continuations or source responses.** Must retain every puncture and the exterior, and justify independently removed cutoffs. The [higher original cutoff theorem](../fzz-p-higher-screening-collisions.md#closed-original-plane-cutoff) derives two power corrections and a logarithmic correction from an original angular integral, proves the remainder integrable, and carries their sum through the original whole-plane cutoff. Must rederive local corrections for each insertion: marking a singular factor and marking a regular factor can produce different divergent terms. The [original marked cutoff audit](../fzz-p-higher-screening-collisions.md#closed-original-marked-plane-cutoff) retains both distinct corrections and the exterior while the punctures shrink independently. Must restore every finite term removed by subtraction before identifying the resulting integral with the original source, as the [complete original polynomial restoration](../fzz-p-higher-screening-collisions.md#closed-original-polynomial-restoration) now verifies.

- **Cannot transfer positivity of an original integrand through finite-part continuation.** Nonnegative cutoff integrals can have a strictly negative limit after their forced divergent counterterms are retained. Must derive and evaluate the complete subtracted source before using any sign in a spectral balance. The [original continuation identity and sign theorem](../fzz-p-plane-screening-cutoff-matching.md#original-gamma-continuation-result) match an actual independently punctured source to its Gamma continuation and prove strict negativity throughout an open parameter strip. Must retain parameter-dependent prefactors, including their signs and zeros, when representing a subtracted source by a positive auxiliary integral. The [higher original remainder audit](../fzz-p-higher-screening-collisions.md#original-fifth-subtraction-and-exponential-lift) proves both the nonnegative admitted weight and its exact prefactor; the restored boundary terms remain separate.

- **Must retain leading normalized residuals before claiming scalar spectral occurrence.** A normalized source can tend to a nonzero constant while its normalized generator grows logarithmically; a scalar multiple cannot match both limits. A unique subtraction that removes this divergence can work at every coefficient parameter and supply no zero selection. The [complete skew-source and forced-correction audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-skew-source-endpoint.md) proves both the obstruction and the exact surviving correction coefficient. Must test the finite residual after cancelling the leading divergence. Exact bounds for the complete signed tails can leave an independent boundary value that any scalar equation must annihilate. The [unit-ground boundary audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-ground-boundary.md) retains that value and proves the stronger all-power decay required of the remaining current.

- **Must control the complete signed tail uniformly in moment order before deducing source nonvanishing from limits of separate modes.** The [high-order source-moment theorem](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-moment-detector.md) retains a nonzero first coefficient while every later normalized mode vanishes; pointwise mode decay alone would not justify that conclusion.

- **Cannot infer convergence of an endpoint moment series from admission of every individual moment.** Factorial-scale retained moments can make its terms fail to tend to zero at every nonzero argument, as the [exact moment-series obstruction](../../5-failed/pw-b-convergent-endpoint-moment-series.md) proves. Must then justify finite asymptotic remainders before identifying endpoint coefficients.

- **Must retain finite boundary values when defining an analytic source through singular parameters.** Explicit subtraction can produce a separately analytic family with nonzero values where multiplication of a totalized singular quotient by its clearing factor gives zero. Must derive the continued source from admitted original integrals and preserve those values before transferring parameter responses. The [signed-source analytic audit](../fzz-p-subtracted-screening-analytic.md) proves separate analyticity, exact pole values and the relation to the original cutoff finite part. The [higher original source audit](../fzz-p-higher-screening-collisions.md#closed-higher-whole-source-continuation) preserves all collision-line values and matches every mixed derivative using an admitted source identity on an open parameter domain. Must retain the derivatives of the explicit subtraction terms when identifying a normalized source response. The [original continued-insertion theorem](../fzz-p-continued-screening-mark.md#closed-original-response) justifies all retained logarithmic integrals and the counterterm derivative before matching their sum to the special-function response. Its [strict source-response lower bound](../fzz-p-continued-screening-mark.md#completed-diagonal-sign-audit) forces a retained insertion to be nonzero throughout an open parameter range. Must derive the complete compensating contribution before imposing stationarity. Must establish parameter responses for the original finite-cutoff family and compare their limits with the continued derivative, retaining every differentiated subtraction term. The [original cutoff derivative and independent-limit theorem](../fzz-p-continued-screening-mark.md#original-marked-cutoffs-and-derivative-interchange) proves this comparison directly for an admitted source family. Cannot substitute a totalized formula at a singular endpoint for its actual one-sided limit. Must justify passage through the original integrals with a common integrable bound. The [complete reciprocal-kernel audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-reciprocal-endpoint.md) proves such a passage in L1 and supplies uniform bounds at every fixed polynomial order.

- **Must bound accumulated conditional means as well as centered innovations in a chronological energy argument.** A cutoff-independent bound for orthogonal innovations can leave all possible unbounded growth in the accumulated conditional means. Must identify the complete source in the original measure and control that remaining part; centering does not supply its estimate. The [physical residue decomposition](../edq-full-covariance-core-attack.md#physical-residue-martingale-bounded-innovations-unpaid-drift-bridge) proves an actual innovation budget and a bounded norm gap to the remaining source.

- **Must retain the measure and complete source before interpreting a vanishing integral.** Two separately nonintegrable source terms can combine into an integrable derivative with zero total mass at every parameter value. Changing the measure can give that same complete response a nonzero mass, while a separate forcing test selects an unrelated parameter. Cannot split the unadmitted pieces or treat such a scalar-test cancellation as an independent spectral constraint. The [actual forcing and measure audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-forcing-measure-classification.md) proves both admission domains, exact masses and the surviving complete arithmetic defect.

- **Cannot infer stationarity of an integrated response from cancellation of its first insertion.** A positive source with an analytic parameter family can have zero normalized first variation while its derivative is strictly positive, even after all original logarithmic insertions and differentiations are justified. Must evaluate the surviving next insertion and establish the complete source balance. The [original all-order marked audit](../fzz-p-plane-screening-marked-derivative.md) proves this cancellation and positive response derivative in its convergence chamber.

- **Must justify integration separately after a countable reconstruction, even when both source and reconstructed output are integrable.** Every positive dilation of a nonzero integrable source can have zero mass while their reconstructed sum is an integrable source with strictly positive mass. The resulting nonzero series/integral defect rules out summability of the reconstruction term norms. Must retain that complete contribution in a balance law; neither zero input mass nor integrability of every term permits discarding it. The [signed endpoint and reconstruction audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-signed-endpoint.md) proves the exact defect and a strictly positive finite norm for the zero-mass source. The [complete logarithmic-current audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-logarithmic-current.md) proves the same phenomenon for the actual generator current. Its admitted difference from the original positive output source has zero total mass; this alone supplies no pointwise cancellation or spectral stationarity.

- **Must separate a convergent source integral from its continuation.** A positive auxiliary-parameter representation can establish whole-space absolute convergence and an exact value while retaining every collision and the tail, provided joint integrability is proved before changing integration order. Cannot carry that convergence or positivity across singular parameter boundaries without deriving the continued source prescription. The [original whole-plane audit](../fzz-p-plane-screening-beta-integral.md) proves the complete integral and its positive convergence chamber.

- **Must distinguish global admission of a signed source from summability of the separate term masses.** An entire pointwise coefficient family and its derivative can have an exact open half-plane of absolute termwise integral admission, with prime terms forcing failure at its boundary. That failure does not exclude an integrable signed sum. Must retain the measure, coefficients and boundary terms when deriving a different global estimate; neither pointwise analyticity nor failure of the absolute estimate settles the signed endpoint. The [exact global admission audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-global-absolute-admission.md) proves the sharp absolute boundary and its positive integrability region. The [actual nonlocal-current audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-frullani-current.md) admits the complete signed response jointly in source and displacement at that boundary. Its exact Abel assembly justifies exchanging the two integrals while preserving a nonzero mass. Must retain that mass in any balance law; the valid exchange does not force cancellation.

- **Cannot infer integrability of a complete source family from repair of one collision.** Changing one singular exponent can make that collision integrable while another remains nonintegrable for every parameter value. A local finite part can still exist after subtracting the regular factor's value at the collision, with its leading power coefficient uniquely forced by convergence. Must retain these local terms and separately prove global continuation and differentiation through it. The [two-collision and original-cutoff audit](../fzz-p-screening-collision-continuation.md) proves the exact convergence boundaries, both local finite parts and the forced coefficient. The [whole-space cutoff audit](../fzz-p-plane-screening-cutoff-matching.md) retains both local terms and the exterior and proves convergence for independently removed punctures. Must still justify equality with any proposed continued family before transferring its parameter responses.

- **Must identify which parameter a complete source current differentiates.** A full arithmetic logarithmic current can be the actual derivative of an entire coefficient deformation, including at zero, while remaining nonzero and differing from the forcing in source-coordinate connection equations. Must derive the parameter transformation, complete forcing and global domain before using that current as a spectral Ward generator. Entire pointwise parameter admission supplies no stationarity or global finite-energy estimate. The [actual parameter-generator audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-parameter-generator.md) proves the complete derivative identity and its surviving nonstationarity. The [complete reflected-generator audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-reflected-frullani.md) admits an actual reflected arithmetic family, its derivative and its joint nonlocal response globally at every complex coefficient parameter, while retaining a nonzero current. That admission excludes no parameter; must derive the spectral identification and an additional full-source balance. Must derive the relation between coefficient and transform parameters from the actual complete integral. A compensating transform-weight change can worsen the source endpoint; cannot transfer an admitted parameter asymptotic through that change without new domain control. The [literal Mellin bridge](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-mellin-bridge.md) proves the joint parameter relation and its sharp coefficientwise admission boundary. Must test the complete signed endpoint after compensating parameter changes: a nonzero normalized endpoint can force actual integral divergence at the new weight, even when the source is entire in its coefficient parameter at every positive coordinate. The [signed-source endpoint audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-source-endpoint.md) proves the sharp actual domain and exact surviving endpoint coefficient. Must retain the actual finite integral exposed by an explicit endpoint subtraction: restoring admission at a former boundary can leave a nonzero parameter response. The [complete subtraction audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-endpoint-subtraction.md) proves the retained derivative and its eventual strict sign from the entire signed source.

- **Cannot cancel a response's nonzero mass by combining globally admitted zero-mass responses with constant coefficients.** The complete residual retains that mass, so its L1 norm cannot fall below the mass's modulus. Must supply a compensating source with the required mass or derive a justified different pairing; rescaling the admitted responses cannot remove this obstruction. The [signed coordinate-endpoint audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-coordinate-endpoint.md) proves both complete source admissions, the exact surviving mass and a positive norm gap.

- **Must test global admission of the complete differentiated source, retaining every accompanying weight and parameter shift.** A coordinate derivative can introduce a shifted family whose compensating coordinate weight preserves the original absolute-admission region. Dropping the weight can unnecessarily shrink the usable domain; pointwise differentiation still supplies no signed endpoint estimate. The [actual coordinate-generator audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-coordinate-generator.md) proves the exact weighted masses and common local derivative bounds for a complete arithmetic family.

- **Cannot count a transformed source variation as an independent zero-location condition when it is the original transform times a nonvanishing analytic factor.** Such a variation preserves both zeros and their local orders. Must evaluate the actual mixed insertion before claiming an additional restriction: joint first and spectral-derivative vanishing can ask only for a repeated zero, without supplying a real-part constraint. The [original marked Mellin audit](../fzz-p-marked-fermi-mellin-ward.md) proves the multiplication law, all endpoint and convergence obligations, and the integrated logarithmic insertion.

- **Must match complete source terms at their first nonzero asymptotic scale before identifying two balance equations.** An arithmetic current and a differential forcing built from the same original source can have different leading behavior at every spectral shift. A nonzero normalized defect cannot be removed by a correction negligible at that scale. Must derive the compensating contribution and its admission from the actual source; a shared source label or reflection covariance does not supply equality. The [leading-source and forcing-defect audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-leading-forcing.md) proves exact surviving coefficients and eventual positivity with the full arithmetic series retained. The [same-input nonlocal audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-same-source-current.md) admits both complete operators on one reflected arithmetic source and proves a nonzero leading coefficient for their difference as its coefficient parameter grows. Cannot replace that difference by an identity across the whole family; must retain its full signed weights and separately justify any spectral restriction or integrated cancellation. Must test the actual integrated leading coefficient before claiming spatial cancellation. A finite nonzero leading mass survives complete admitted integration and bounds the scaled full-source L1 norm away from zero, as the [complete spatial-mass audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-same-source-mass.md) proves.

- **Must retain which source variables are shared before multiplying and normalizing integrals.** With the same positive unit-mass marginal law, a product integrated over one shared parameter can have a strict mixed response, while a product of separately integrated factors has zero mixed response everywhere. Matching the marginal laws does not justify replacing one assembly by the other. Must derive the actual joint source law and preserve its parameter identification. The [coupling and shared-source audit](../fzz-p-fermi-coupling-exchange.md) proves this distinction and the exact surviving first-response and mixed-response zero sets.

- **Must account for a nonzero reconstructed source term in any complete cancellation.** A signed arithmetic forcing can have a convergent, strictly positive sum on every positive dilation ray, with a positive lower bound from each prime and positive values arbitrarily far out. Such forcing cannot be discarded as identically zero. Must derive the cancelling contribution from the complete source; positivity of the reconstructed sum supplies neither pointwise positivity nor stationarity. The [actual forcing reconstruction](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-forcing-positive-reconstruction.md) proves this distinction with all arithmetic coefficients retained.

- **Cannot infer failure of an arithmetic construction from failure of a stronger sufficient source class.** A source outside a two-sided decay class can still have a nonzero Möbius reconstruction and convergent complete arithmetic currents, proved from an explicit one-sided bound. Must verify the actual source's convergence and preserve its dilation coefficients and full derivative source. Exact inversion and convergence do not imply a vanishing Ward response. The [actual-source extension](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-mobius-current.md) proves this distinction with all half-density factors retained.

- **Must retain real mixed derivatives after pairing complex-conjugate source terms.** A pair can have an identically real normalized first response while its derivative has a strict sign away from one spectral label. Reality alone supplies no stationarity. Must evaluate the surviving derivative and derive its vanishing, or its complete source balance, independently. The [integrated marked-response audit](../fzz-p-marked-radial-replica-response.md) proves such a response and its exact zero set, including differentiation of the original integral; it does not enforce full-source vanishing.

- **Cannot infer zero forcing from exact fixation of the original source.** A singular weighted reflection can fix its original source and match the corresponding complete forcing at every complex shift, while even the unshifted forcing remains nonzero. Must derive vanishing from an additional full-source law before using reflection covariance to exclude zeros. The [two-orientation forcing audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-reflected-source-forcing.md) proves this survivor and the distinct nonzero forcing mismatch for the opposite gain orientation; it also checks the source class required by the transported arithmetic identity.

- **Must retain charge-dependent coupling factors after subtracting source divergences.** A positive subtracted source can have exact integral `exp(-s*u)*Gamma(s)/b`, with positive slope `b` and charge `s=-3/2+i*p/b`. Its logarithmic coupling response is `-s`, whose real part is `3/2` even at zero momentum. Must account for this surviving charge in the complete source balance before imposing stationarity. The [charged-source audit](../fzz-p-subtracted-external-gamma.md) proves the integral, normalization and an explicit original cutoff prescription; it does not determine a complete physical counterterm.

- **Must preserve both complete forcing and admissibility when using uniqueness to compare reflected responses.** A necessary series term-decay condition can exclude every nonzero homogeneous solution and give uniqueness for a forced equation, while exact reflection sends the original source outside that same condition. Must derive the reflected forcing and any required boundary correction before invoking uniqueness to identify partners. The [arithmetic-current audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-euler-current-domain-uniqueness.md) proves this obstruction on an explicit nontrivial domain; that domain excludes all homogeneous shifts, including critical ones, so it cannot itself realize the Xi divisor.

- **Must distinguish a source Ward equation from a vanishing response.** A strictly positive unit-mass source can have a nonvanishing transform `C` satisfying `partial_u C/C=-i*p/b` at every real momentum `p`, with fixed positive slope `b`. The derivative then vanishes exactly at zero momentum, but normalization and the Ward equation do not impose that vanishing. Must derive the additional stationarity condition from the complete source before using it to eliminate spectral labels. The [original coupling-insertion audit](../fzz-p-coupling-insertion-gamma.md) proves the integral, Gamma normalization, exact coupling phase and derivative in Lean.

- **Cannot infer a selective spectrum from finite energy in a positive source-derived norm.** One fixed source-square norm can admit a nonzero solution at every complex shift of the same differential connection, together with finite energy for its connection image. Reflection can pair opposite shifts with equal total energy throughout. Must derive the physical operator domain and an additional arithmetic restriction before using such admission to eliminate off-line zeros. The [all-shift solution and energy classification](../../3-formalized/pw-b-source-square-shifted-modes.md) proves this failure of selection without claiming a closed-domain spectrum or adjoint.

- **Must differentiate the finite part and retain its boundary contribution.** Subtracting an infinite, parameter-independent source volume need not make the parameter derivative vanish. For the radial kernel `exp(-A*exp(b*x))`, with positive slope `b`, ordinary integration above a finite lower cutoff exists exactly when `A>0`. Subtracting the radial length leaves finite part `-(log A+gamma)/b`, whose logarithmic-coupling derivative is `-1/b`; the differentiated cutoff integral has the same limit. Must specify the continuation outside the convergence chamber and retain the boundary term before claiming a vanishing Ward response. The [radial normalization audit](../fzz-p-radial-zero-mode-ward.md) proves these statements in Lean, without determining a complete source counterterm.

- **Must prove that admitted homogeneous solutions vanish in the exact proposed measure.** Finite energy and reflection invariance alone do not imply this. A strictly positive solution excluded by ordinary square integrability can have finite positive energy when weighted by the square of the original source, while remaining fixed by reflection. Bounded mean energy likewise need not force vanishing. Must classify and exclude the surviving homogeneous solutions before using an energy identity to eliminate zeros. The [original-source-square energy classification](../../3-formalized/pw-b-source-square-horizontal-energy.md) proves these distinctions; it does not identify a physical Hilbert space or establish an adjoint or Fredholm index after changing the measure.

- **Must control differentiated normalization before assigning a sign to a logarithmic comparison.** Two finite, strictly positive compact source integrals can have a logarithmic difference whose mixed parameter derivative ranges over every real value as the integration scale changes. A vanishing first source variation need not remove the direct mixed variation. Must retain that source term and the scale comparison; positivity of the integrals alone does not orient their relative response. The [two-insertion source and compact-integration audit](../fzz-p-two-marked-source-contact.md) proves the exact coefficient and scale law in Lean. It does not determine the sign of a complete source with additional terms.

- **Must distinguish a completed relation from a determined observable.** A dense linear source admission with a scalar readout can have its joint graph dense in the entire source–output product. Then every output is admitted above every source input; minimizing squared output norm selects zero without identifying the original readout. An RH argument using a domain enlargement must derive the required output agreement in addition to existence of the enlarged domain. The [arithmetic energy-graph audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-energy-graph-saturation.md) proves this complete ambiguity exactly when its retained scalar coefficient is nonzero. This full-product conclusion concerns the linear graph, not a positive-cone restriction.

- **Must retain parameter variations when a normalized boundary becomes cyclic.** A complete source can have vanishing limiting exchange defect while its first normalized variation remains nonzero and agrees with the original physical boundary's variation. Must distinguish the regulator limit from the source's fixed spectral parameter; endpoint cancellation does not identify a reader at the original position. Cannot demand vanishing first variation merely from a zero endpoint value. The [full-source Abel audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-critical-abel-reader.md) proves this behavior and first-order physical agreement for the complete integral source, including its retained zero multiplicities.

- **Must compare variations that retain the complete source correction.** Leading values and first boundary variations can agree while the second boundary variations, together with one scalar first variation, determine every coefficient of an absolutely summable diagonal correction. Cannot infer exact agreement from the lower-order match, or claim a finite diagonal change removes a correction whose nonzero coefficients survive beyond every finite bound. Must distinguish diagonal recovery from recovery of the whole operator, and measurements at every integer dilation from prime tests alone. The [second-variation audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-abel-second-comparison.md) proves these distinctions for the actual complete source. Its nonzero second discrepancy also occurs at critical multiplicity-top sources, so vanishing of that discrepancy is not an RH condition.

- **Must retain the actual source cutoff when a fixed measurement is compared across scales.** A complete complex tail can retain a nonzero normalized response at one fixed arithmetic label after every finite diagonal repair. Cannot infer cancellation from termwise decay or replace that family by one uncut measurement. Must prove the whole-tail estimate on the original cutoff operator and preserve the order of regulator and cutoff limits. The [fixed-measurement tail audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-fixed-six-tail-reader.md) proves this source retention, including the first nonzero zero jet; it supplies no independent critical-line selection.

- **Must distinguish joint-limit damping from annihilation of the original source.** A vanishing limiting measurement can coexist with a nonzero actual top-jet defect at every sufficiently late cutoff. Must retain the relative regulator/cutoff rate and prove agreement with the fixed physical reader before using such a limit to exclude zeros. Cannot silently replace that reader by a rate-compensated diagnostic. The [complete original-source crossover](../../3-formalized/route-l-regulator-depth-crossover.md) proves an exact attenuation law, retains the adjacent zero/nonzero internal pair along every positive regulator path tending to zero, and removes finite diagonal repairs pointwise before limits.

- **Must retain relative decay when applying a nonlinear readout to vanishing measurements.** Two actual measurements can both tend to zero while their quotient determines the fixed physical value at the unchanged source parameter. Must prove the limit of the complete readout before replacing either input by its endpoint. Recovery of a physical value still does not prove its vanishing. The [actual adjacent-measurement audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-adjacent-depth-physical-reader.md) constructs that recovery and proves discontinuity of the literal totalized decoder at the zero pair.

- **Cannot infer annihilation of a difference merely from equal nonlinear readouts of its two terms.** Invariance under swapping factors does not supply compatibility with subtraction. Must prove the relevant subtraction identity or an independent complete-source balance. The [native product audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-native-wold-cyclic-readout.md) proves that two actual product families, their commutator and the decoded subtraction discrepancy all reconstruct the same physical boundary scalar. Its vanishing remains exactly critical-line placement; the equality of the product readouts does not establish that placement.

- **Must identify the physical reader's symmetry sector when using symmetry to prove cancellation.** A source fixed by an involution can have a nonzero boundary that changes sign under the induced transform. Vanishing on transform-fixed tests is compatible with nonzero values on other tests. Must prove that the original physical reader belongs to the annihilated class, with its source data retained, before applying that cancellation. The [actual source and parity audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-complete-transgression-physical-balance.md) exhibits both cancelling and detecting tests for the same retained nonzero boundary.

- **Cannot infer radial balance from exact reciprocal transport.** An identity exchanging two nonzero normalized responses as `u` and `u⁻¹` does not force `‖u‖=1`. Must derive equality of their squared norms from the complete source, retaining the first nonzero derivative at the zero and both normalization factors. The [literal Fourier-source audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-burnol-reciprocal-window-incidence.md) identifies that squared-norm difference with the original physical defect; its vanishing remains exactly critical-line placement.

- **Must retain normalization costs when using orthogonality.** Two actual sources related by a unitary operator have equal norms, but normalizing them by their respective first nonzero derivatives can destroy that equality. Must prove the required comparison after both normalizations, or derive the compensating contribution from the complete source. Cannot transfer vanishing of the real pairing of the original sum and difference to the normalized pair. The [actual-source estimate](../../3-formalized/route-l-real-polarized-source-balance.md) proves a strict quantitative normalization bias and shows that equality after normalization is exactly critical-line placement.

- **Must check symmetry on discarded measurement kernels.** A source component killed by the original derivative can become visible after Fourier transport. Cannot infer covariance of a minimum-norm representative merely because projection preserves the original measurement. Must retain the transported measurement or prove that its kernel is invariant. The [literal source witness](../../3-formalized/route-l-jet-kernel-fourier-transport.md) has zero original leading derivative and a quantitatively nonzero Fourier derivative on the strict right half, with both vectors already in the algebraic source.

- **Must distinguish the specified original source from a source chosen to fit measurements.** Exact interpolation can realize both balanced and unbalanced measurement pairs inside the admitted source space while preserving every lower derivative at an actual zero. Cannot deduce a balance law for the original source merely from that freedom or from Fourier unitarity. The [joint source interpolation](../../3-formalized/route-l-two-prime-joint-jet-source.md) proves this distinction at the unchanged zero and exact multiplicity.

- **Must retain domain changes in minimum-energy comparisons.** A canonical representative can preserve both original and transformed measurements, commute with the transform, and remain coherent across changing source domains while retaining the original normalization bias. Cannot infer a uniform measurement bound or physical cancellation from those properties. The [canonical-source law](../../3-formalized/route-l-coherent-joint-source-transport.md) proves the exact domain-dependent norm cost; at a hypothetical strict-right zero, fixed nonzero readings have arbitrarily small minimum norm as the aperture shrinks.

- **Must derive the correction class from the actual source before proposing a singular reader.** Removing a boundedness assumption does not remove an arithmetic obstruction when the complete integral values themselves force a reciprocal leading term plus a summable correction. Must retain the leading coefficient and prove every integral's admission; an undefined finite head cannot supply a physical correction. The [literal integral-column audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-integral-column-law.md) derives the correction class from complete spectral decay and proves its exact summability and prime-test classification, without a bounded Hilbert-reader assumption.

- **Must check what physical information is lost when removing a nonsummable leading term.** For a reciprocal vector tail plus an absolutely summable remainder, a bounded linear probe has summable output exactly when it annihilates the leading vector. If a separating family of physical probes all annihilates its image, that image is zero. Cannot call this removal faithful without accounting for the lost vector; a nonzero remainder can still survive. The [bounded physical-probe audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-bounded-sonin-probes.md) proves the exact source decomposition, summability criterion, and detecting physical evaluation.

- **Cannot infer loss of divisor information from norm decay or finite head removal.** A complete source tail can tend to zero in Hilbert norm while retaining every zero's first nonzero source jet at each sufficiently late stage. A singular, source-forced normalization can recover a nonzero limit. Must control that normalization and identify the actual physical reader before passing from source norm convergence to spectral vanishing. The [strict-tail audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-strict-tail-xi-jets.md) proves this behavior for one and the same source, including its complete integral and exact norm law. The [complete tensor audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-tensor-remainder-xi-jets.md) shows that the retained divisor jets can survive in the full tensor remainder too; both leading–tail cross terms must be included.

- **Must retain the full family of arithmetic readouts when testing a correction.** For an absolutely summable complex family, its sums over multiples of every positive integer determine every coefficient. If all nontrivial multiples sums vanish, only the unit coefficient remains. Cannot infer this conclusion from the total sum or from prime tests alone without another argument. A summable correction cannot turn a nonsummable retained tail into such a finitely supported family. The [diagonal reconstruction audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-cyclic-diagonal-reconstruction.md) applies this principle to a fixed physical reader without a positivity assumption.

- **Must control all overlaps when aggregating local arithmetic tests.** A common bound on the total absolute test weight applied to each source coordinate makes every summable correction produce summable test outputs. Such a correction cannot cancel a forced nonsummable output family. Cannot carry this conclusion to a boundary parameter without rechecking that bound: arbitrarily many tests can accumulate on one coordinate there. The [prime-incidence audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-prime-incidence-repair.md) proves the full comparison and its sharp parameter threshold, including complex signed corrections.

- **Must prove admission of products before using cyclicity of a series-defined reader.** Two actual source operators can each have a convergent diagonal sum while their product does not. A parameter at which the exchange multiplier becomes one can lie outside that product's convergence domain. Must establish a common product domain and retain the exchange multiplier before claiming boundary cancellation. The [literal operator-domain audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-gibbs-operator-domain.md) proves this failure for an actual source chain, together with a sharp admission criterion and the exact weighted exchange law.

- **Must exclude escape of source mass before inferring coefficient vanishing from a decaying weighted readout.** A positive source-only exhaustion can retain unit trace and a nonzero singular limit while losing every finite range of labels; its weighted output can tend to zero for every fixed coefficient. Must recover the coefficient through an independently justified comparison or retain nonzero mass where the detector weight stays bounded below. The [source escape and retention audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-prime-escape-retention.md) constructs this phenomenon and bounds every finite-range mass by its reciprocal-weighted energy.

- **Must control signed corrections when passing a fixed observable through a singular limit.** For detector weights tending to zero outside finite sets, coefficientwise convergence to a finite target and a uniform bound on the sum of absolute coefficients preserve the weighted output. A correction carried where the detector weight has magnitude at most `epsilon` contributes at most `epsilon` times that coefficient bound. Must derive the required control from the actual source; cancellation of the total trace does not supply it. The [signed marginal audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-signed-marginal-stability.md) proves this comparison law and an exact growth obstruction for unscaled coherent sources. A [complete-source correction](../../3-formalized/route-l-complete-coherent-star.md) can have zero graph limit and bounded marginal variation while retaining a nonzero weighted output. Must identify the actual marginal limit; graph convergence does not supply it.

- **Must verify joint consistency after assembling locally consistent corrections.** Membership of every summand in an admissible class does not imply membership of their sum. An explicit finite assembly can preserve positivity, unit trace and a singular source limit while losing the marked mass and making the fixed readout vanish; one jointly consistent correction obeys a retention bound excluding that behavior. The [positive assembly audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-positive-transpose-assembly.md) proves both the assembly and its eventual failure of the joint condition. Cannot replace that condition by separate checks of the pieces.

- **Must justify approximate consistency in the norm actually used by the argument.** Agreement of weak source limits does not imply a small error in total absolute source coefficients. An explicit positive assembly remains uniformly separated from every jointly consistent correction in that stronger norm, even though the singular graph limits agree. The [full-source distance audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-coherent-distance.md) proves the separation uniformly over all finite profiles and supports. Must derive the physical approximation topology; exact nonmembership alone neither selects it nor excludes weaker approximation.

- **Must budget total sign error when extending a positivity argument to signed approximations.** Unit trace, bounded contracted variation and the original singular source limit can coexist with a vanishing fixed readout and a strictly positive total negative mass. Must derive the required sign-error control before contraction from the actual source. The [negative-mass audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-negative-mass-repair.md) proves a quantitative retention interval, a signed readout bound and a persistent negative-mass cost for an explicit escaping correction; bounded variation after contraction does not supply that control.

- **Must test every surviving native mode after resummation.** An absolutely convergent local sum of complete fields sharing one scalar transformed value can give a nonzero square-integrable source whose actual operator residual is nonzero. A known native eigenmode detects the target mismatch multiplied by the source's component in that mode, and gives a quantitative residual bound. Must prove the operator action after summation; admission alone does not justify an infinite operator interchange. Removing the detected component leaves a separate residual equation. The [complete resummation and native mean audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-resummed-source-mean-obstruction.md) proves this behavior and the exact surviving centered decomposition.

- **Must test cancellation of the actual asymptotic exponents in the original norm.** A finite sum of distinct complex exponential modes is square integrable on the positive half-line exactly when every nonzero coefficient has negative real exponent. Equal growth rates with different oscillations do not cancel a forbidden mode. A separately admitted complete remainder cannot repair this failure. Equal transformed spectral values do not imply equal asymptotic powers. The [complete finite source audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-finite-quarter-lattice-packets.md) proves the exact criterion and its native half-density transport. Must distinguish weaker local resummation from completion in the original norm: finite errors against an admitted proposed limit must themselves be admitted before invoking norm convergence or a closed operator.

- **Must differentiate a changing testing map together with its source.** When the source's constant and linear terms vanish, the cubic tested coefficient retains the first variation of the test against the complete quadratic source. Must solve that full quadratic equation before discarding this contribution; cancellation of selected components is insufficient. Once the quadratic source vanishes, a regular change of the testing map supplies no independent cubic cancellation. The [actual metric-path audit](../../3-formalized/hb-action-source-density.md) proves this with the volume and both raised tensor indices retained. The bulk identity does not remove boundary terms or establish extinction.

- **Must control coupled singular limits before discarding a vanishing factor.** A current integral tending to zero can still have a fixed positive product with a divergent reciprocal-amplitude integral. Two complementary arcs of a closed original-source curve charge their common squared chord to the complete resistance/current product, giving a lower bound of four times that chord. Must use the period of the complete vector, which can exceed the period of its scalar integrands. The [closed-period source audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-closed-period-current-product.md) produces a strict period-scaled product floor at every positive height, including common-zero heights. This does not imply positive limiting current, bounded resistance or a prescribed fraction of total area mass.

- **Must retain temporal switching in source-coupling tests.** When the response residue factors into a spatial source pairing and the actual switching transform, pole survival requires both factors to be nonzero. Nonzero spatial excitation alone does not discharge the temporal factor. Exact mode normalization and a nonvanishing observation profile can remove detector-dependent zeros, while leaving these source factors to control. The [complete radial-source reduction](../../3-formalized/hb-fundamental-radial-source-moment.md) identifies all factors and proves the resulting observation-independent criterion.

- **Must distinguish whole-trace vanishing from nodal vanishing.** A second-order source equation propagates zero value and zero normal derivative; zero values alone require additional boundary information. For the actual cuspidal source below the quarter threshold, a whole zero horizontal trace annihilates its complete boundary form and flux, contradicting the boundary-square identity and strictly positive tail mass. Every slice therefore has positive integrated squared mass, with a positive source-dependent minimum over each compact height band. This cannot be promoted to pointwise zero-freeness, a bound on reciprocal amplitude, or exclusion of a common flat node. The [original slice-rigidity audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-maass-slice-rigidity.md) proves the Cauchy propagation and all positivity assertions from the original source.

- **Must combine the complete source before judging dependence on a cutoff.** An original pairing and its retained correction can each depend on the cutoff while their exact difference is independent of it. Must carry the transition integral, terminal boundary term and every source coefficient through the same calculation. The [complete angular-source audit](../../3-formalized/hb-effective-angular-source.md) proves such cancellation and identifies the surviving coefficient with explicit original analytic factors. Its possible vanishing remains a source-incidence question; removing cutoff dependence does not establish nonvanishing.

**Must justify persistence across slices before charging positive line current to area.** A positive contribution on one slice supplies positive area mass only after its integrated value is proved to persist over a nonzero parameter interval. For a C² periodic finite vector source, the complete horizontal-current limit is continuous in height even at common-zero heights: horizontal second derivatives vanish at almost every common zero, the signed acceleration quotient is almost everywhere continuous in height, and compact source bounds justify both limit interchanges. Thus a positive line current gives a source-dependent positive rectangle bound for every sufficiently small regulator. Zero limiting band mass forces zero current at every height in the band. These facts do not supply a uniform lower bound or prescribed fraction of total mass. Audited source: [current height continuity](../sqb-current-height-continuity.md), `SelbergMaassCurrentHeightContinuity.lean`.

- **Must distinguish denominator simplicity from source incidence.** After retaining the complete source, a response can have an exact simple-pole term plus an analytic remainder while its pole coefficient still vanishes. For an analytic numerator, a nonzero denominator derivative fixes the normalization and excludes higher poles; it does not prove the retained numerator nonzero. Must evaluate every surviving source factor before claiming a nonremovable response pole. The [complete physical Laurent audit](../../3-formalized/hb-physical-self-response-laurent-coefficient.md) proves the exact source-factor criterion, including both endpoint moments and every cutoff correction.

- **Must resolve the equality case before promoting nonnegative energy to a strict bound.** For the complete original period current, a zero limit forces pair/phase vanishing and a common real radial derivative; every common zero then has zero first derivative in the tested direction. Actual zero means exclude this case on a zero-free period because the normalized source would be constant. The [source-rigidity audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-zero-period-current-rigidity.md) therefore leaves a specific alternative containing a common zero with vanishing first derivative. Must exclude or quantitatively control that alternative using the original source; radial rigidity alone does not establish its absence.

- **Must identify the full integrated limit before controlling a singular product.** Exact periodic boundary cancellation can represent a concentrating current by a signed source/second-derivative quotient with an integrable source majorant. This gives a finite nonnegative current limit without deleting original zeros. Multiplied by divergent resistance, a strictly positive current limit forces product divergence; bounded nodal products require zero limiting current, after which relative rates still matter. The [complete current-limit and joint-product audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-original-period-current-limit.md) proves these laws and removes the regulator from the source comparison on its explicitly zero-free two-period domain. It supplies no uniform quotient-area lower bound.

- **Must control reciprocal-amplitude costs at original source zeros.** A linear upper amplitude bound at a zero forces logarithmic divergence of the regulated reciprocal integral along a path through it. On a zero-free compact period, the source itself supplies a positive floor and a finite reciprocal limit, with an explicit regulator error. Must distinguish such a source-specific floor from a uniform bound over varying heights, levels or sources. The [original-period classification](../../3-formalized/sqb-original-period-resistance.md) proves boundedness and finite-limit equivalence to zero-freeness, and divergence equivalence to a common zero. Divergent resistance alone does not determine the limit of its product with current energy.

- **Must distinguish a resistance-weighted line estimate from an area lower bound.** Periodicity can reduce every integer-length path to one complete period while leaving a quadratic length cost and a reciprocal-amplitude resistance. A lower bound on resistance times a current line integral does not remove either factor or establish an unweighted area estimate. Must prove the required passage with the original mean, regulator and common-zero contributions retained. The [complete source-path and two-period identities](../../3-formalized/sqb-unipotent-current-paths.md) make these costs explicit in Lean.

- **Must identify the averaging family when claiming uniformity.** Four alternating complete subgroup factors give a variance bound with coefficient 16 independent of the finite group size. The subgroups themselves can grow with the parameter. Must retain that changing family and prove its comparison to the original differential energy before using the algebraic coefficient as an analytic bound. This does not identify a bound for a fixed pair of individual generators. The [full-block comparison and original-source reconstruction](../../3-formalized/sqb-congruence-unipotent-blocks.md) are proved in Lean at every positive congruence level, including composite levels.

- **Must distinguish a finite comparison constant from a uniform one.** Reaching every element of each finite group with two generators gives a finite variance bound, but the average of squared shortest-word costs grows when group sizes grow. Choosing different complete word representatives cannot lower that cost. Must retain parameter dependence before using such a construction globally. Conversely, divergence of one proved coefficient does not show that the optimal comparison constant diverges. The [exact finite comparison, counting bound and actual parameter growth](../../3-formalized/sqb-congruence-generator-cost.md) and [retired uniform-coefficient inference](../../5-failed/sqb-uniform-word-cost-comparison.md) are closed in Lean.

- **Must retain what an averaging step removes.** For a finite unitary group average, the lost squared norm is exactly the squared norm of the complementary vector. An averaged source may vanish; then its complement is the full source. A differentiated complex source also retains mixed mean/complement brackets, and its phase contributions add before squaring. Must preserve these terms and the original normalization before claiming a quantitative lower bound. The [original-source averaging and current audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-congruence-source-mixing.md) proves the exact variance, current and regularized-curvature decompositions, including the zero-average case; it supplies no uniform spectral comparison.

- **Must check which vectors and approximate families a spectral transformation preserves.** If a continuous function takes a selected value at only one point of a bounded self-adjoint operator's real spectrum, its exact eigenspace and bounded approximate eigenfamilies equal those of the original operator at that point. Equal continuous spectral zero sets give equal bounded approximate kernels without a spectral gap or differentiability assumption. Changing spectral values cannot supply the missing native concentration. The [exact kernel audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-entire-forcing-kernel.md) and [continuous concentration audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-native-spectral-concentration.md) prove both laws; the latter also recovers actual native-domain unit approximate vectors with norm error tending to zero from any successful entire family. These statements classify occurrence requirements and do not independently produce occurrence.

- **Must control residuals relative to the norm of the actual response.** Unit forcing vectors can have both their full response and their unnormalized residual tend to zero, even at a target excluded by self-adjointness. Positivity of each normalization denominator is insufficient when those denominators tend to zero. Must retain the exact residual quotient or prove a sufficient lower bound for its denominator. The [native unit-forcing construction](../../3-formalized/sqb-native-spectral-concentration.md) gives a quantitative covariance bound, vanishing numerators at every complex target, and actual normalized responses whose residual at i stays at least one. The [excluded unnormalized inference](../../5-failed/sqb-unnormalized-forcing-occurrence.md) retains these positive results.

- **Must justify operator-domain membership before using a pointwise equation spectrally.** Smoothness and finite source norm alone do not identify the domain of a closed operator. When using approximation, must identify both the source limit and its complete operator-image limit, including cutoff derivative and boundary terms. The [original-source graph audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-original-source-native-graph.md) proves both norm limits and retains the full nonzero forcing in the resulting operator equation; graph admission does not remove that forcing. The [normalized cutoff-family audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-entire-forcing-kernel.md) proves that actual graph approximants can converge to a strictly positive limiting residual. Must distinguish approximation of the original source from approximation of an eigenvector.

- **Must distinguish a vanishing scalar pairing from a vanishing source vector.** A complete retained forcing can be nonzero in the original Hilbert space while its absolutely convergent pairing with the original field is zero. Must prove that the tested quantity controls the required vector or residual before using its cancellation. The [original cutoff-forcing audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-original-cutoff-forcing.md) proves both facts for every actual Xi copy; a complementary boundary moment detects the nonzero forcing. That pairing is bilinear, so it cannot be read as a squared Hilbert norm.

- **Must prove zero-specific spectral occurrence after constructing a self-adjoint operator.** A dense closed domain, a real lower bound, and a bounded resolvent do not identify where the original complex zero data occur. The original modular source gives such an entire-transform operator and actual unit domain packets at every real cusp frequency. At a Xi zero `rho`, the ordinate-selected packets have exact limiting residual `|cosh(4*pi*Im(rho))−F((rho/2)(1−rho/2))|`, which vanishes exactly for critical copies. This is the defect of those constructed packets, not an asserted infimum over the whole domain. Admission and normalization do not remove the horizontal mismatch. Even a correction retaining the full complex parameter can produce a nonzero unit domain vector while leaving an explicit forcing term. Must prove that term vanishes or becomes negligible; preserved symmetry and finite norm do not establish it. The [original modular source construction](../../3-formalized/sqb-original-modular-cusp-subtraction.md) proves the complete correction, nonvanishing, domain admission and exact remaining residual. Evidence: `entireOperator_selfAdjoint` and `entireOperator_re_inner_lower` in `RHTower.SelbergScatteringEntireOperator` ([operator audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-entire-native-operator.md)); `xiEntirePacket_residual_tendsto` and `xiEntirePacket_residual_zero_iff` in `RHTower.SelbergScatteringEntireXiPackets` ([source transport and audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-entire-cusp-occurrence.md)).

- **Must account for symmetry and norm together when reweighting a source.** A multiplier can change pointwise growth while breaking a required symmetry. Restoring an invariant density can exactly restore the original density and its divergent norm, eliminating that apparent gain. Must check the full measure and the equality case: an integrable remainder cannot change admission of the leading source term. Must prove the transformed equation, symmetry, and domain estimates together before inferring spectral occurrence. The [full-source quarter-shift audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-quarter-shift-source-equation.md) proves an explicit rapidly decaying forcing, failure of scalar modular invariance, and a sharp classification of compensating power densities; it does not exclude other transformations. The [complete cusp-norm classification](../../3-formalized/sqb-full-source-cusp-norm.md) proves the exact admission threshold with every Fourier mode retained and shows that the unique invariant compensation restores divergence. Even a bounded source-derived normalization with exact dilation intertwining and a summable complete remainder can retain a nonzero logarithmic boundary contribution. Must check the transformed boundary and its physical evaluation separately; the [full-source scalarization audit](../../3-formalized/route-l-full-gamma-shell-flattening.md) proves this distinction. Must retain the actual variable-change Jacobian when deriving a pairing from opposite rates, and distinguish uniqueness within a prescribed rate-balance class from a source law forcing that class. Must prove the complete integral operator identity and its domain separately. The [reversible-density audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-reversible-density.md) proves the exact Jacobian, density classification and integrable source geometry. Must compare transported and native cutoffs before taking an adjoint limit: shrinking cutoff intervals can retain a nonzero logarithmic contact when their integrand is singular. The [literal contact theorem](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-adjoint-cutoff-contact.md) proves the retained limit and its vanishing remainder bound. Must test an asserted adjoint on the actual admitted source vectors: positive balanced rates can coexist with a strictly nonzero paired generator defect. The [source-mode audit](../../3-formalized/pw-b-gamma-adjoint-source-test.md) proves such a defect together with its finite strictly positive cross-mass.

- **Must distinguish resonance incidence from Hilbert-space occurrence.** For every actual Xi zero `rho=beta+i gamma`, the constructed modular operator `A=1−Delta_H` has exact greatest lower unit residual `|gamma|(1−beta)/2` at native scattering energy `1+(rho/2)(1−rho/2)`. Explicit normalized cusp packets approach this floor, and every unit vector in the original operator domain satisfies the lower bound. It is positive even at critical zeros, where it equals `|gamma|/4`. Thus these literal scattering energies cannot be approximate eigenvalues in that domain; a proposed occurrence construction must change the literal relation. Evidence: `xi_residual_isGLB`, `xiNativePacket_residual_tendsto`, and `xi_scattering_no_approximate_eigenvectors` in `RHTower.SelbergScatteringNativeResidual` ([proof and audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-native-domain-residual.md), Lean).

- **Cannot extend a polynomial obstruction to all entire changes of spectral parameter.** An entire function defined by `F(1/4+w²)=cosh(8πw)` sends every critical scattering energy `3/16+t²/4+it/4` to the real value `cosh(4πt)`, and sends every real native energy to a real value at least −1. For each actual Xi zero `rho`, equality `F(lambda)=F((rho/2)(1-rho/2))` with real `lambda` holds exactly when `Re rho=1/2` and `lambda=1/4+(Im rho)²/4`. The entire function is constructed; existence of those matching real parameters for all zeros still needs proof. Evidence: `casimirTransform_not_polynomial`, `real_native_preimage_iff`, and `xi_real_native_preimage_iff` in `RHTower.SelbergScatteringEntireTransform` ([proof and audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-entire-casimir-transform.md), Lean).

- **Cannot obtain a real spectral encoding of all zeta zeros by a fixed nonconstant polynomial of their modular-scattering energies.** The energy at scattering parameter `s=rho/2` is `s(1-s)`; for a critical-line zero `rho=1/2+it`, this equals `3/16+t²/4+it/4`. For every fixed nonconstant complex polynomial P, the absolute imaginary part of P evaluated on this parabola tends to infinity. Unconditional critical-line zeros supply genuine scattering poles beyond every height and beyond every prescribed distance from the entire real axis. Thus even bounded-error transport to a real spectrum must leave this fixed polynomial class; only real constants are real on all actual Xi copies. Evidence: `real_on_all_xi_copies_iff` and `exists_critical_pole_far_from_real_above` in `RHTower.SelbergScatteringPolynomialReality`, declared in `SelbergScatteringPolynomialXiObstruction` ([proof and audit](../../3-formalized/sqb-polynomial-scattering-separation.md), Lean).

- **Must distinguish a temporary increase from a violation of the claimed bound.** A complete trajectory can stay below one fixed ceiling while some states admit only increasing next steps. Stepwise contraction is a stronger requirement and cannot replace the intended global estimate without proof. A telescoping signed balance can budget all consumed square costs through initial energy and accumulated charge, but the charge still needs its own bound. The closed scalar ordering theorem `exists_scalarSubsetMergeOrder` in `RHTower.SignedScalarMergeOrder` bounds every prefix by the larger endpoint even when the remaining steps increase; the [actual-source arity balance](../ccm-fu-arity-charge.md) retains every interacting child cost. Interacting source terms still require their own bound; finite trajectory experiments do not supply it.
- **Must distinguish a uniform state error from accumulated step errors.** If a transformation compares each complete state directly with its original state, the same error allowance can cover arbitrarily many stages; it need not be summed over the history. Every intervening action still needs control. The [actual-source mass-rounding transformation](../ccm-fu-late-order-rounding.md) preserves all middle insertions and bounds all intermediate late-prime states, so any fixed relative order of isolated late primes suffices whenever some uniformly quadratic order exists. This permits a larger constant and does not establish the missing interacting-prime bound.
- **Must distinguish feasible compensation from available compensation.** A step that incurs an immediate cost to reduce a later cost must fit both stages below the claimed ceiling. This can impose an upper and lower bound on its size, even when the final result improves. A sharp continuous optimum does not supply an actual unused operation of the required size. The [complete-source two-step classification](../ccm-fu-joint-prime-minimax.md) proves the exact admissible interval and unique optimal amplitude; continuing arithmetic availability remains unproved.
- **Cannot cancel the signed mass a retained source must still carry.** For cells with signed masses F and original absolute masses A, weighted energy `Σ F²/A` is exactly the square of total signed mass divided by total absolute mass, plus a nonnegative variance. Cancellation can reduce only the variance while those totals are fixed. Removing a negative source part increases the signed mass the remaining cells must carry; an upper-bound proof must budget that forced component even if every remaining cell has the optimal constant signed density. The [actual-source mass-balance obstruction and sharp equality classification](../../5-failed/ccm-fu-corrected-coarea-mass-balance.md) exclude a corrected common-mass energy bound under every complete prime order.
- **Cannot repair a forced intermediate comparison loss by changing processing order.** If a proposed majorant contains the product of consumed mass and remaining mass, with total mass T and single-step increments at most D, every complete order has a prefix costing at least `(T²-D²)/4`. When this exceeds the target scale, arbitrary interleaving cannot rescue that majorant. Must retain a finer comparison, such as the original squared sizes of isolated source terms. The [actual-source obstruction and exact diagonal correction](../../5-failed/ccm-fu-coarea-all-orders-mass-split.md) prove this loss for every complete prime order while leaving the underlying signed-energy bound open.
- **Cannot reverse invariant growth by subdividing or reordering its increments.** If an allowed family of operations preserves an exact positive growth rate per unit mass, splitting the same mass into smaller steps or changing their order leaves the total increase unchanged. Must change the state governing that rate or budget the full increase. The [actual coarea source law](../../5-failed/ccm-fu-coarea-mass-envelope.md) proves both an exact invariant rate and its unconditional positive growth at cofinal cutoffs.
- **Must budget the initial norm before invoking contraction.** For finite real source coefficients, the minimum of `Σ c_d²/w_d` over nonnegative probability weights, with zero weight allowed only on zero coefficients, is `(Σ |c_d|)²`, attained by weights proportional to absolute amplitude when that mass is positive. This can exceed the original diagonal `Σ c_d²` by a factor proportional to the number of active indices. Monotone conditional energy must recover the lost scale through a proved quantitative dissipation or mass comparison. The [actual divisor-source normalization theorem](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-positive-normalization-cost.md) proves matching order-M cost bounds, including strictly positive weights, without an RH hypothesis.
- **Must exclude even one off-line zero.** A density estimate allowing a sparse exceptional set, however small, is not yet RH. The missing step is elimination of the exceptions, not another interpretation of the proportion.
- **Must prove that a classification covers every zero.** Different classes may have different proofs of critical-line membership. The classes need independent definitions, exhaustive coverage, and a proved line conclusion for each class. A partition of primes or of arithmetic coefficients does not itself partition the zeros; any such connection requires proof, including the terms involving several groups. A class-specific contradiction at hypothetical off-line zeros remains a legitimate target.
- **Cannot obtain selection from reflection alone.** Reflection pairs opposite horizontal displacements; it does not identify each zero with its reflected partner. Symmetry-compatible countermodels still have off-line zeros. Evidence: F06, F14.
- **Must retain the information the conclusion needs.** A transformation that forgets real parts, multiplicities, or signs must recover them by a proved identity before it can constrain those data. A self-adjoint operator listing zero ordinates does not locate the original complex zeros. An energy assigning zero weight to an observable needs a separate bound for that observable, even when the energy vanishes at completion. The [actual-source arity energy](../ccm-fu-arity-charge.md) vanishes after complete prime processing irrespective of the final signed sum. Evidence: F10, F13, F664; `mobiusDivisorArityEnergy_of_complete`.
- **Cannot count reversibly transformed coordinates as additional zero constraints.** An exact forward transform and inverse preserve the same source information. Their existence supplies no sign or magnitude bound on the recovered values. A bound on the number of surviving indices also leaves their source weights uncontrolled. Must prove the decisive comparison for those actual values. Evidence: `stationaryComplexBudget_eq_sum_firstHarmonic` and `stationaryComplexBudget_mobius_reconstruction` in `ArithmeticStationaryFourierDilation`; [formal scope](../../3-formalized/edq-stationary-frequency-dilation.md); [exact coprime-column transport](../ccm-fu-coprime-column-transport.md).
- **Cannot bound the raw even theta moments by one geometric sequence.** For the positive theta density representing Xi, `m_n=2∫₀∞x^(2n)Φ(x)dx` has `m_(n+1)/m_n→∞`. Every distant interval contributes a positive lower bound with arbitrarily large geometric base. A model with bounded moment ratios therefore needs a proved transformation before its moments can represent these raw source moments. Evidence: `RHTower.tendsto_thetaCuspRawEvenMoment_ratio_atTop` in `ThetaRawGramGrowthObstruction`.
- **Must distinguish finitely many derivatives from a complete derivative hierarchy.** An exact source Fourier identity and a nonzero exponential moment window can make the entire signed jet at one point determine a global sign. Completeness supplies no positivity at unproved orders. Tail bounds proved separately at each fixed derivative order need a further uniform argument before quantifiers over order and center can be exchanged. A strict lower block can coexist with a negative next block; an all-order argument needs a proved propagation or compensation law. Evidence: `RHTower.exists_thetaCuspNativeSixthRankBoundary` in `ThetaCuspNativeSixthRankBoundary`; `RHTower.completeHankelCone_rawMoment_iff_of_expDecay` in `UnwindowedHankelSignCone` and `thetaCuspCenterMixedSignedJetCone_iff_riemannHypothesis` in `ThetaCuspCenterMixedSignedJetCompleteness`.
- **Must prove the head is invertible before reducing a larger matrix to an inverse-based scalar comparison.** A strictly positive head permits an exact Schur-complement test, but does not supply the sign of the new scalar. A merely semidefinite head needs separate treatment at its kernel. Positivity for every shape at one finite packet size still leaves larger sizes to control. Evidence: [strict six-dimensional source and cluster positivity, followed by the exact unproved successor integral](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-sixth-rank-and-successor-source.md).
- **Must bound an omitted infinite source after every derivative and product used in the final comparison.** A finite retained sum must be linked to the full source by an exact remainder identity. Products create linear cross terms and a quadratic remainder; all must survive the estimate. Factoring before absolute values can preserve cancellations needed for a useful bound, but the resulting error bars still require a proved sign margin. Must recheck the order range of each majorant: higher derivatives can require a different polynomial radius even when the source and its exponential decay are fixed. The [complete derivative, correction and matrix error theorem through twelve](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-even-four-complete-error.md) retains all these terms; the interior sign margin remains to be proved. Evidence: [complete two-atom derivative and reflected-product bounds](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-two-atom-middle-source-control.md) and their [proved transfer to a global compensated sign](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-global-odd-compensation.md).
- **Cannot infer failure of an integrated sign from failure at individual integration points.** A derivative with proved integrability and vanishing boundary values can change local signs while preserving the exact integral. Must prove the resulting comparison over the whole integration domain. A complete source-specific correction can repair a finite integrated block even when the uncorrected pointwise block fails. Evidence: the [global compensated source comparison and positive actual mixed odd block](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-global-odd-compensation.md); the all-order hierarchy remains unproved.
- **Must control the remaining interior after proving positivity near both integration endpoints.** A fixed correction with zero integral can give strict positivity near the origin and past a finite threshold while leaving a compact interval unresolved. Those endpoint signs alone do not determine the integral. An existential threshold is not an explicit error certificate; a proved explicit outer endpoint still leaves the interior to control. Evidence: [complete-source compensation outside a compact gap](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-even-four-compact-gap.md) and its [explicit outer margin](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-even-four-explicit-tail.md).
- **Must prove the lifespan of a differential source before using an escape contradiction.** A state compatible with a local equation may be impossible on a solution defined throughout a complete ray. Such completeness can force a strict barrier and quantitative energy bounds, but the equation and its global solution must both come from the actual source. Evidence: the [literal-source drift barrier and decreasing energies](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-global-modular-energy-compensation.md) are unconditional; their [complete determinant comparison](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-global-odd-compensation.md) is proved, while all-order signs remain open.
- **Must compare a source approximation with a proved margin in the same normalization.** A small error alone gives no sign. In a determinant, retaining an exact cofactor can give a much sharper error ceiling than taking absolute values of every monomial separately. Every other entry error and every product of errors must also be bounded. Rescaling coordinates can make a uniform perturbation bound useful, but the exact determinant scaling and the cofactor of any reserved entry must be carried back to the actual matrix. Evidence: `signedEvenHankelThreeDet_middle_error`, the [complete-source determinant transfer](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-global-odd-compensation.md), and the [balanced four-dimensional reserve with exact normalization](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-even-four-explicit-tail.md).
- **Must track sign changes when tuning an exact correction.** Increasing a correction coefficient can improve one region while worsening another. A unique sign transition can select different rigorous coefficient bounds on the two regions, but each resulting inequality still needs proof. A proved bracket for the coefficient can make endpoint control uniform over that bracket; it does not close the intervening source comparison. Evidence: `exists_thetaCuspCenterCompensationSource_transition`, the odd coefficient comparison, and the [complete-source even rational calibration and common endpoint regions](../../3-formalized/clg-ttf-even-compensation-rational-band.md).
- **Must derive the decisive property for the fixed arithmetic object.** Positivity, boundedness, recurrence, or regularity taken as an RH-equivalent input is an endpoint, not a source proof.
