MUST-PRESERVE-MULTIPLICITY-IN-HORIZONTAL-SIGNS — Must retain full analytic multiplicity when turning critical-line energy into a horizontal sign argument. A nonnegative critical-line squared norm can have negative reflected horizontal contact at an odd-order zero: the contact order is twice the zero multiplicity, and its leading sign alternates with that multiplicity. Cannot infer two-sided interior nonnegativity from the boundary energy. Must accommodate the negative neighborhoods forced by actual simple critical zeros above every height. The [exact contact classification](../../3-formalized/edq-completed-horizontal-contact.md) is closed in Lean, including a common magnitude and sign neighborhood for every arithmetic channel having the same reflected pair.

MUST-RETAIN-THE-FULL-QUADRATIC-FORM — Must retain every cross term when testing whether complex channel combinations remove an obstruction. If the actual prefix streams are independent and their energy weights have one strict sign, every nonzero finite complex combination has that same strict sign. Cannot treat a change of channel coefficients as an independent annihilation law. Must justify passage from a finite positive certificate to the complete current by retaining and controlling all endpoint terms. The [full mixed-current Gram law and explicit finite-prefix detection](../../3-formalized/edq-mixed-current-gram.md) are closed in Lean.

MUST-KEEP-THE-ENDPOINT-DEFECT — Must control the order of source completion and endpoint limits. Paths whose individual endpoints are zero can converge on every fixed positive prefix to a nonzero unit endpoint. Cannot exchange those limits or discard the resulting boundary defect. Positive finite-prefix bounds can force the complete current to escape every finite bound; even bounded returns along a cofinal sequence then require an independent source proof. The [literal unit endpoint defect and cofinal current classification](../../3-formalized/edq-mobius-current-escape.md) are closed in Lean.


MUST-CONTROL-GROWING-PREFIX-WINDOWS — Must distinguish decay of a scalar coefficient from control of energy accumulated over expanding prefixes. A vanishing coefficient can preserve a nonzero prefix boundary throughout every fixed logarithmic-power window; same-sign energy over those windows can grow faster than every logarithmic power. Cannot infer a logarithmic ceiling on the complete response from that scalar decay alone. Must prove the ceiling independently, even when it is required only along an unbounded sequence. The [uniform growing-window bounds and quantitative current escape](../../3-formalized/edq-mobius-log-growth.md) are closed in Lean.

MUST-DISTINGUISH-SIGNED-MASS-FROM-COVERAGE — Must prove complete source coverage separately from large balanced consumption. A partial ordering can consume arbitrarily many linear units of both positive and negative source mass under one uniform quadratic energy ceiling while leaving entire groups of source operations unprocessed. Must retain the removed coordinate squares when matching signs: they can pay a crossing overshoot and bound every prefix of the selected batch. Cannot infer a bound for the complete response without controlling the remaining operations and their changes to future available source. The [actual growing signed-batch construction](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-growing-signed-prime-batches.md) is closed in Lean and exhibits this distinction without numerical premises.

MUST-CONTROL-COMPLETION-AFTER-LOW-ENERGY-PREPARATION — Must distinguish existence of low-energy intermediate states from a bound on their complete evolution. Even after every prime below a growing threshold has been processed, the energy of an intermediate state and every individual next operation can fit arbitrarily small quadratic budgets while the accumulated remaining signed contribution still requires its own estimate. Must prove both a bounded path reaching the state and control of completion when using such a construction as a complete-path argument. The [unconditional actual terminal-core construction](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-small-terminal-core.md) and the exact terminal endpoint criterion are closed in Lean; the final signed arithmetic bound remains open.

MUST-PRESERVE-THE-CRITICAL-TAIL-WEIGHT — Must retain the critical square-energy weight when converting endpoint cancellation into a bound for a complete tail. Pointwise tail estimates can leave a divergent harmonic sum; simultaneous weighted row and column control can give a finite bound for the full output energy. Must retain the transfer constant's dependence on distance to the critical line. Cannot infer growth control of the arithmetic input from its summability at each fixed cutoff or from a cutoff-independent transfer constant. The [complete arithmetic primitive and critical Hardy–Schur transfer](../../3-formalized/edq-primitive-energy-transfer.md) are closed in Lean.

CANNOT-330 — [paper; exact test law in Lean, corrected 2026-09-08] **A finite-aperture Weil positivity certificate cannot see zeros near the line, and cannot beat direct verification.** Setting: constrained positivity at aperture `S` (`Q_S(u) ≥ 0` on `V₀`, `supp u ⊆ [−S/2, S/2]`), with the exact zero side `Q_S(u) = Σ_ρ û(ρ) conj û(1−ρ̄)`. Exact test law (Lean, `ConstrainedWeilTwistedOddTest.suzukiScrewSmoothForm_twistedOddTest`, in the corpus normalisation `Q(cos(γ*x)ψ) = Σ'_q −(1/4)(A_q + A'_q)²/z_q²` with the second moment at frequency `Im ρ_q + γ*` negligible): for `u = e^{−iγ*x} φ` with `φ` real, smooth, supported in `[−S/2, S/2]`, put `Ψ_ρ = ∫ φ e^{(a_ρ + i(γ_ρ − γ*))x}`, `a_ρ = Re ρ − ½`; then `Q_S(u) = −Σ_ρ Ψ_ρ²` for odd `φ` and `+Σ_ρ Ψ_ρ²` for even `φ`. On-line zeros contribute `|Ψ_ρ|² ≥ 0` in both cases; an off-line pair `{ρ, 1−ρ̄}` contributes `∓2Re Ψ_ρ²`, of opposite signs for the two parities. Hence the certificate for both parities gives `|Re Σ_off Ψ_ρ(γ*)²| ≤ Σ_on |Ψ_ρ(γ*)|² ≤ C S² log|γ*|` for every centre `γ*`. (i) Isolated case [Lean, `ConstrainedWeilNegativityWitness.re_suzukiScrewSmoothForm_twistedOddTest_le_of_topExcess`]: if the excess of `ρ₀` exceeds that of every other zero by a margin `δ` (all other copies have `|Im z_q| ≤ a₀ − δ`), then `Re Q(cos(γ₀x)ψ) ≤ −(9/50)Φ₊²/γ₀² + e^{2a(a₀−δ)}(∫|ψ|)² S₂` with `S₂ = Σ_q |z_q|^{−2}`, so the form is negative once `e^{2δa} ≫ a₀² a² γ₀² S₂`: a top excess gap `δ` is detected at aperture `a ≈ (log γ₀ + log a)/δ`; with the local zero count in place of `S₂` the rate for `δ = a₀` improves to `a₀ ≤ (log log|γ₀| + log S + C)/S` (paper). (ii) General case: off-line zeros clustered within ordinate distance `≲ a₀` can cancel the odd-test signal (the Gram matrix of `{e^{iγ_ρ t}}` on an interval of length `1/a₀` is ill-conditioned once more than `1/a₀` zeros lie in a unit window), and the local count `≤ K log|γ|` per unit interval bounds the achievable cancellation only through a Turán power-sum inequality, giving `a₀ ≤ C (log|γ₀|)(log(a₀ S))/S`; no argument gives the rate of (i) unconditionally. The earlier multiplier "ringing envelope" argument is invalid: the pointwise partial-sum expansion of `−ζ'/ζ` is only formal, and the smoothed prime sum with the odd autocorrelation weight has the opposite sign. Consequences: (iii) the certified aperture `S = 1` carries no zero information; (iv) to beat the classical zero-free region at height `T` an aperture certificate needs `S ≫ log T · log log T` in general (primes up to `T^{C log log T}`), i.e. more than direct verification, and `S ≈ 2 log log T` only if the off-line zeros are isolated; (v) any source proof of aperture positivity with `S → ∞` is a proof of RH, and with `S ≍ log T log log T` at least a zero-free strip below height `T`; (vi) this is the quantitative form of the Connes–van Suijlekom ground-state wall (`ConnesXiGroundStateLimitWall`): aperture-`S` ground states are real-zero approximants whatever off-line zeros below the detectable excess exist, so locally uniform convergence of their transforms to `XiC` is never a statement about a fixed finite prime set.

MUST-LOCATE-EXCESS-IN-INTERFERENCE — Must locate growth beyond every logarithmic power in the aggregate cross contribution once the complete diagonal has a fixed logarithmic ceiling. Cannot attribute that excess to omitted diagonal mass or infer a full energy ceiling from positivity of the Gram form. Must retain both coefficient signs and every cross pair in the remaining arithmetic comparison. A head/tail split can remove a logarithmic loss in individual diagonal estimates without supplying any cancellation between different components. The [complete source Gram, first-power diagonal bound, and forced cross-term escape](../../3-formalized/edq-mobius-gram-cancellation.md) are closed in Lean; no off-line zero is asserted to exist.

MUST-COMPARE-THE-FULL-GROWTH-SCALES — Must retain the cutoff power when comparing asymptotic upper and lower bounds. Cannot infer a subpower ceiling from saving every fixed logarithmic power relative to a linear cutoff: that saving can coexist even with fixed positive sublinear power growth. Must retain the exponent-dependent constants and thresholds; a theorem for every fixed exponent does not allow the exponent to grow with the cutoff without additional uniform control. Must balance an extended-prefix estimate against its complete complementary tail before claiming a full-source bound. The [unconditional full arithmetic energy and absolute cross-sum savings](../../3-formalized/edq-mobius-global-log-saving.md) are closed in Lean; the stronger ceiling needed for zero exclusion remains open.

MUST-RETAIN-THE-WEIGHTS-AFTER-AVERAGING — Must prove a quantitative comparison before replacing a weighted energy by a uniform cycle average. The same deterministic periodic source can have cycle variance beyond every logarithmic power while its actual weighted energy divided by that variance tends to zero. Cannot transfer a logarithmic ceiling between them merely because they use identical coefficients or both are positive quadratic forms. Must retain residue-dependent weights and the growth of the common period. A growing set of components orthogonal to every other component contributes positive cycle variance that the remaining coefficient signs cannot cancel. The [exact projection, uniform-average obstruction, and quantitative positive survivor](../../5-failed/edq-period-average-growth-obstruction.md) are closed in Lean.

MUST-259 — [Lean] **If the largest excess `Θ = max_ρ |Re ρ − ½| > 0` is attained at a zero `ρ₀` with ordinate `γ₀`, then the constrained Weil form of the twisted plateau family `cos(γ₀ x)ψ_a(x)` is negative on a recurrent set of apertures with exponential size: there are `c > 0` and `L₀ > 0` such that every aperture window `[A, A + L]`, `A ≥ ½`, `L ≥ L₀`, contains an aperture `a` with `Re Q(cos(γ₀x)ψ_a) ≤ −c e^{2Θa}`.** `ConstrainedWeilApertureWindowMean.exists_aperture_twistedOddTest_neg_of_attainedTop (hΘ : 0 < Θ) (q₀) (hq₀ : |Im z₀| = Θ) (hall : ∀ q, |Im z_q| ≤ Θ)`, with `c = (1/8)κ(Θ)²(γ₀² − Θ²)/(γ₀² + Θ²)²` and `L₀ = 4D/c + 1`, `D = Σ_q decayNorm_q`. Mechanism (nine modules, all zero-side, no RH input): (i) the exact ray-term law `Q(cos(γx)ψ) = Σ'_q −(1/4)(A_q + A'_q)²/z_q²`; (ii) for the odd plateau profile `ψ_a = τ(4u)τ(4(a−u)) − (u ↦ −u)` every twisted moment is an *exact exponential polynomial in the aperture*, `A(b,μ) = e^{ca}κ(c) + ρ(c) − e^{−ca}κ(−c) − ρ(−c)`, so `e^{−2Θa}·(ray term of q)` is an exponential polynomial with exponents in the closed left half-plane; (iii) window-mean law for such polynomials: `|∫_A^{A+L} Re eval − L·Re constTerm| ≤ 2·decayNorm`; (iv) the constant term is `−(1/4)κ(Θ)²/z_q²` exactly for the copies at the top height with ordinate `±γ₀`, and `0` for every other copy; its real part is `−(1/4)κ(Θ)²(γ₀² − Θ²)/(γ₀² + Θ²)² < 0` because every Xi zero has `|ordinate| > 9/2 > ½ > Θ`; (v) `Σ_q decayNorm_q < ∞` from the coefficient bounds `‖κ(c)‖, ‖ρ(c)‖ ≤ M e^{|Re c|/4}/‖c‖`, the exponent-sum gap for copies far from the centre, finiteness of the near copies, and `Σ_q |z_q|⁻² < ∞`; (vi) interchange of `Σ'_q` and the window integral by the uniform bound `4B²/|z_q|²`. Consequences: (a) this removes the isolation/gap hypothesis of CANNOT-330 (i): **clustered off-line zeros cannot hide the top excess from the constrained form once one averages in the aperture**, because clustering only affects the non-constant exponentials, whose window means vanish at rate `2D/L` uniformly in the window position; (b) it is the Weil-form analogue of MUST-256 (attained top ⟹ unbounded lags on log-dense sets), with the aperture playing the role of `log n` and the plateau family the role of the integer sampling; (c) it is a quantitative, hypothesis-light special case of Bombieri–Yoshida (`riemannHypothesis_of_polarGroundEnergy_nonneg`), which needs positivity at *every* aperture and gives no aperture or rate; here the negativity recurs in every window of length `L₀` and grows like `e^{2Θa}`; (d) receiver status unchanged: the theorem locates where positivity fails given an off-line zero; it supplies no source-side sign.

MUST-CONTROL-CANCELLATION-AT-THE-ABSOLUTE-SCALE — Must track component growth and cancellation precision together. Cannot replace an absolute energy ceiling by norm ratios tending to one or normalized real inner products tending to minus one: growing vectors can approach opposite directions while their sum still grows. Must control the squared relative residual multiplied by the squared component norm at the required cofinal cutoffs. The [actual weighted cancellation law](../../3-formalized/edq-weighted-prime-packet-cancellation.md) proves asymptotically opposite arithmetic components and every fixed logarithmic saving in their relative squared residual unconditionally; the required absolute ceiling remains open.

CANNOT-331 — [paper, from the Lean chain of MUST-259] **Aperture averaging trades the clustering obstruction for the non-attainment obstruction; the two are complementary, and neither averaging nor pointwise witnesses replace the Landau (Laplace-pole) engine.** For an attained top excess the window mean of `e^{−2Θa} Re Q(cos(γ₀x)ψ_a)` isolates the top copies because only they carry a zero exponent; every other copy (clustered or not) is a non-constant exponential whose window mean is `O(decayNorm/L)`. For a non-attained supremum `Θ` there is no copy with a zero exponent at the normalisation `e^{−2Θa}`, and at any lower normalisation `e^{−2θ_j a}` the copies with excess in `(θ_j, Θ)` are growing exponentials: their window contribution is `≲ L e^{2(Θ−θ_j)(A+L)}(A+L)² Σ_{k>j} |z_k|⁻²`, while the isolated constant is `≍ κ(θ_j)² L/γ_j²`; with the sharp window length `L₀ ≍ (log γ_j)³/θ_j` (near copies dominate the decay total) the comparison holds only when the near-top zeros are sparse (`γ_{j+1} ≳ γ_j log γ_j`) and their excesses converge super-fast (`Θ − θ_j ≲ (log γ_j)^{−3}`), exactly the regime of the abstract counterexample of the lag ratio law. In the complementary dense regime the window mean carries no sign. Hence: (i) the attained/non-attained dichotomy is intrinsic to every averaging (Bohr-type) argument, in the lag variable and in the aperture variable alike; (ii) the non-attained case is exactly what the Widder–Landau engine of Bombieri–Yoshida handles, and it handles it only because it uses *one-sided* control of the whole family rather than a mean; (iii) a criterion built on window means of a single explicit family can therefore reach `RH ∨ ¬attained` (`not_attainedTopExcess_of_twistedPlateau_nonneg`, Lean) but reaches RH itself only through a Laplace continuation in the aperture variable, never through more averaging.

CANNOT-332 — [paper; literature Maynard–Pratt arXiv:2206.11729 Cor. 5 and BGSTB Acta Arith. 214 (2024) Thm 1; Lean for the budget: `RHTower.tendsto_montgomeryForm_div_leadingOrder` gives `F(T^α,T) = (T/2π)·α log T·(1+o(1))` for every fixed `0<α<1`, over all zeros of ξ with multiplicity and no hypothesis on real parts; the spaced-configuration density count is Lean: `RHTower.eventually_card_spaced_offline_le` — for fixed `0<α<1`, `Θ>0`, `δ₀>0` and all large `T`, if the zeros with `T^{α(Re ρ−1/2)} ≥ Θ` are pairwise 10-spaced in ordinate then `#{0<γ≤T, Re ρ ≥ 1/2+δ₀} ≤ (αT log T + 25600Θ²L_T²(T/10+1))/T^{2αδ₀}`] **The failure of RH cannot be sparse.** Off-line zeros at displacement `δ = β − 1/2` that are isolated — Maynard–Pratt half-isolation, or, in the unconditional Montgomery form `F(x,T) = (2/π)∫|Σ_ρ x^{δ+iγ}/(1+((t−γ)+iδ)²)|²dt` (a mean square, hence positive), pairwise ordinate spacing `≥ 10` among all zeros with `T^{δ} ≥ K log³T` — satisfy the density-hypothesis count `N ≪ T^{1−2δ} log T`, because a spaced sub-configuration contributes `≥ π T^{2δ}` per zero to the mean square while its cross terms with the rest are bounded by the absolute far field, which the local zero count `O(log T)` controls. Averaging the form over the scale `x = T^α`, `α ∈ [1/2,1]`, sharpens this: a finite cluster confined to a unit window can cancel only by killing `≍ εL·width` phase-weighted moments, which the local count `N(t+1)−N(t) ≤ 0.43 log t` caps, so finite clusters are detected with weight `≥ T^{1.88δ}` per zero; an infinite vertical progression is excluded by the argument principle (`|S(t)| ≤ 0.112 log t + …` caps a progression with spacing `2πc/log T` at length `1.41c/(1−c)`), and a single finite progression block is detected through its two endpoints with energy fraction `≍ 0.036/(δ log T)`; what hides completely is a **periodic (or near-periodic) array of finite vertical-progression blocks** — block spacing `2πc/log T`, `0.37 ≲ c ≤ 1/(1+2πδ)`, block length `≤ 1.41c/(1−c)`, period `≍ 0.44/δ` — whose zero counting measure has Fourier support at `|ξ| ≥ log T/c`, so the whole array has `O(1/δ³)` effective zeros in the Montgomery passband (exact lattice cancellation between blocks). Maynard–Pratt's short-scale one-sided detector catches the bottom zero of each block only when real parts are rigid (their Hypothesis F). A producer that reads only bandwidth-`≤1` statistics (pair correlation, zero-detecting polynomials of length `≤ T`) cannot exclude these arrays; the corpus's lag function is coherent on them only at `n ≥ T^{1+2πδ}`, where its prime side is the RH-equivalent lag ceiling. Excluding them needs primes beyond `T^{1+2πδ}` at height `T`, or a source law that sees a periodic block structure of the zero measure at frequencies `≥ log T/c` with real parts varying at scale `≳ 1/log T`. Correction (paper, 2026-09-07): sharp blocks (constant displacement) and their periodic arrays are detected — Parseval on the comb factor gives each block `O(1)` effective zeros instead of its length `K ≤ 0.036 log T/δ`, so they obey the pair-form density count `N ≪ T^{1−2αδ} log²T/δ`. The true evaders are **tapered AP blocks**: `K ≈ 0.16 w log T` zeros on an exact progression of spacing `2π/((1+Δ) log T)` over a window of length `w ≥ 2`, with an analytic displacement profile of height `δ₀` vanishing at the ends; by Poisson summation and a saddle point their contribution relative to on-line zeros is `O(1)` at every frequency `ξ ∈ [log T/4, log T]` once `Δ ≥ 2δ₀√α/w` (density excess `≤ 0.08 log T`, within Trudgian's bound). No periodicity and no `α`-averaging loophole (the earlier cluster-detection claim was Taylor-moment counting, not a lower bound); only the AP precision `T^{−αδ_j}` and BGSTB's `F(α) → α` (blocks must be a vanishing proportion of all zeros) constrain them, and entire-function rigidity (order, type, functional equation, reality on the line) does not: a Hadamard product over any such zero set has all four properties. So the pair form at bandwidth `≤ 1` cannot yield the density hypothesis for general configurations, and its inverse-theorem open core is moot. Mechanism in Lean: `GaloisForLFunctions.HTGeometry.tsum_lattice_eq_integral_of_fourier_vanishes` — for a Schwartz `f` whose Fourier transform vanishes at all frequencies of modulus `≥ 1/s`, `Σ_{n∈ℤ} f(x + ns) = s⁻¹ ∫ f` for every shift `x`: a configuration on an arithmetic progression of spacing `s`, weighted below the Nyquist band, is exactly its own continuum average for every test of exponential type below `1/s`. Card `1-in-progress/offline-isolation-dichotomy.md`.

CANNOT-333 — [paper, 2026-09-07; Lean receiver `RHTower.RiemannGaussian.gaussianArithmeticGoodWidthsUnbounded_iff_riemannHypothesis`] **A positivity criterion built on an analytic test family cannot resolve a displacement `δ` at height `γ` with less prime data than the family's complex growth prescribes, and every known certificate stops at the trivial edge.** For the Gaussian family `e^{−w(t−c)²}` (RH ⟺ good widths unbounded), an off-line zero at `(δ,γ)` is seen at width `w` only when `e^{wδ²}` beats the on-line background `log γ/(2√(πw))`, i.e. `wδ² ≳ log log γ`; the prime side at width `w` is a Gaussian-smoothed von Mangoldt polynomial whose mass sits at `n ≈ e^{w}`, of smoothed trivial size `√w·e^{w/4}` against the required ceiling `log γ/(2√(πw))`. Hence widths `w ≤ 4 log log γ − O(log log log γ)` are good by the trivial bound and certify only `Re ρ < 1`; the Vinogradov–Korobov region is the width `4 log log γ·(1 + O((log γ)^{−2/3}(log log γ)^{−1/3}))`, a vanishing relative margin above trivial; every width `(4+ε) log log γ` is a zero-free strip `Re ρ ≤ 1 − ε/16 + O(ε²)`, unknown for every `ε > 0`; RH is `w → ∞`. The incremental content of the Gaussian order is a power saving `N^{ε/(4(4+ε))}` over the smoothed trivial bound for prime polynomials of length `N = (log γ)^{4+ε}` at height `γ`, uniformly in the height: the zero-free-strip problem, not an RH-equivalence at any finite width, but open at every width beyond the trivial edge. Mean values over the height control all but an exceptional set; the criterion is pointwise, and Halász–Montgomery large-value bounds are void at the required ceiling (`V ≈ log γ ≪ N^{1/2}`). Evaders of the pointwise criterion on any bounded range of widths: off-line clusters balanced at the frequencies `2wδ` (a pair at ordinate distance `π/(2wδ)` hides at one width; tapered AP blocks hide on a range), so one certified width yields a zero-free region only for isolated zeros and only for `γ ≤ exp(exp(w/4))`. Certifying widths from primes is the Gaussian analogue of verifying RH to a finite height, with doubly exponential reach `exp(exp(w/4))` per prime length `e^{w}`. Worst configuration (tapered AP block of CANNOT-332): invisible until `w ≍ log γ/(4δ)`, an instance of the `1/δ` law of `harmonic-invisibility-and-the-one-over-delta-law.md` with scale `log γ/4`; isolated zeros are seen earlier, at `log log γ/δ²`.

CANNOT-334 — [paper, 2026-09-08] **A bandwidth-`≤ 1` pair statistic cannot read zero multiplicities without RH: the diagonal-dominance kernels lose positivity at displacements of order `1/log T`.** Montgomery's deduction "pair correlation `F(α) = α` on `|α| ≤ 1` ⟹ `Σ_ρ m_ρ² ≤ (4/3 + o(1)) N(T)` ⟹ at least `2/3` of the zeros are simple" tests the form against a kernel `r` with `r̂ = (1−|α|)_+` and uses `r = sinc² ≥ 0` to drop the off-diagonal pairs. Off the line the pair `(ρ, ρ')` enters with the complex argument `r(θ − ic)`, `θ = (γ−γ') log T`, `c = (δ+δ') log T`, and `Re r(θ − ic) = Σ_k (−1)^k c^{2k} r^{(2k)}(θ)/(2k)!`; at every double zero `θ₀` of `r` this is `−(c²/2) r''(θ₀) + O(c⁴) < 0` (for `sinc²` at `θ₀ = 2π`, i.e. one mean spacing: `−0.0010` at `c = 0.2`, `−0.0255` at `c = 1`). So the unconditional pair-correlation theorem (BGSTB; Lean `RHTower.tendsto_montgomeryForm_div_leadingOrder`) yields no unconditional simple-zero proportion by this route, and any repair needs a kernel `r ≥ 0` with `supp r̂ ⊆ [−1,1]` whose twisted extension `w(z)·r(−i z log T)` (`w(z) = 4/(4−z²)` the Montgomery weight) has nonnegative real part on the strip `|Re z| < 1`; no such kernel exists for the range that matters: for zeros at fixed positive displacement the argument is `c = (δ+δ') log T → ∞`, and for every `r̂ ≥ 0` with `1` in its support the tilted weight `r̂(α)e^{αc}` concentrates at `α = 1`, so `Re r(π − ic) < 0` for all large `c` (Lean: `GaloisForLFunctions.HTGeometry.eventually_integral_tilt_cos_neg` — `∫_{−1}^{1} g(α) e^{αc} cos(πα) dα < 0` eventually, for every `g ≥ 0` with positive mass on every top window). Two off-line zeros at ordinate distance `π/log T` (half a mean spacing) and the same displacement therefore enter every admissible kernel negatively, and the pair form is multiplicity-blind unconditionally. Only the regime `δ + δ' ≲ 1/log T` is left, where it needs kernels with real zeros of order `≥ 4`, and a statement confined to that regime is an RH-type hypothesis.

MUST-COORDINATE-THE-INITIAL-DEFECT-AND-TAIL — Must check the complementary error on the same cutoffs selected to suppress the initial defect. Cannot transfer smallness of one scalar initial defect to the remaining arithmetic field without a quantitative comparison. Must retain their signed cross term and every later block, or prove a complete-tail transfer. The [exact first-block energy decomposition and full-error comparison](../../3-formalized/edq-harmonic-cutoff-tail.md) are closed in Lean; finite cutoff-selection evidence is kept separate from every infinite-limit claim.


MUST-TRANSPORT-DISCRETE-SIGNS-TO-CARRIERS — Must establish a discrete one-sided condition on the actual continuous carrier before applying a positive-integral continuation theorem. Exact affine interpolation in a monotone clock can transfer endpoint signs without a new positivity assumption. Must prove absolute convergence and retain every clock factor when replacing that carrier by a Dirichlet expression; an initial-half-plane identity still requires a continuation identity before it can exclude poles. The [literal source-to-Mellin transport](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-retained-source-mellin-transport.md) closes these transport and interpolation steps in Lean. The [finite-field source certificate](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-finite-retained-directional-control.md) additionally closes positive continuation and finite directional control; cofinal preparation and energy costs remain separate obligations.

MUST-260 — [Lean] **If the largest excess `Θ = max_ρ |Re ρ − ½| > 0` is attained, the pole-vanishing ground energy of Suzuki's restricted Weil form decays to `−∞` at least exponentially with rate `2Θ`: there are `c' > 0` and `B₀` with `μ₀(B) ≤ −c' e^{2ΘB}/B³` for every aperture `B ≥ B₀`.** `ConstrainedWeilPolarGroundDecay.polarGroundEnergy_le_neg_exp_of_attainedTop (hΘ : 0 < Θ) (q₀) (hq₀ : |Im z₀| = Θ) (hall : ∀ q, |Im z_q| ≤ Θ)`, with `μ₀ = ccmRestrictedWeilPolarGroundEnergy`, the corpus's canonical RH-equivalent functional (`μ₀ ≥ 0 ∀B ⟺ RH`, and `μ₀` has at most one zero aperture `B*`, positive below and negative above). Mechanism: the pole-vanishing multiplier `D = ∂² − ¼` applied to the twisted plateau test `cos(γ₀x)ψ_a` gives a pole-vanishing source `DΦ_a` at aperture `a + 1` whose CCM quadratic is the ray energy of the twisted test weighted by `(z_q² + ¼)²`; second-order plateau coefficient bounds (`‖κ(c)‖, ‖ρ(c)‖ ≤ M₂e^{|Re c|/4}/‖c‖²`, from two integrations by parts on the smooth-transition edge, which is flat at both ends) make the weighted ray terms uniformly bounded by a summable majorant; the aperture-window mean of `e^{−2Θa}·Re Q(D(cos γ₀x ψ_a))` converges to `−(#top copies at ±γ₀)·c_D`, `c_D = (1/4)κ(Θ)²((γ₀²−Θ²) + ½ + (γ₀²−Θ²)/(16(γ₀²+Θ²)²)) > 0`; the norm of the source is `≤ 2a(|γ₀| + 4K + (2a+1)/4)²`; antitonicity of `μ₀` spreads the recurrent bound to all large apertures. Consequences: (i) the unique zero aperture satisfies `B* ≤ B₀ = 3/2 + 4D_w/c_D + 1`, an explicit function of the top zero and of `Σ_q |z_q|⁻²`: the strict-domain dichotomy of the O lane is now quantitative; (ii) the ground energy cannot fail "slowly": failure of positivity is exponential with the exact exponent `2Θ`, so any source-side estimate showing `μ₀(B) ≥ −o(e^{2ΘB})`… is void, while `μ₀(B) ≥ −C e^{εB}` for one `ε` would already exclude every attained excess above `ε/2`; (iii) this is the Weil-form counterpart of the prime growth exponent law MUST-255 (`a_p` grows like `p^{Θ−½}`), in the aperture variable; (iv) receiver status unchanged: the theorem describes how positivity fails, not why it should hold.

MUST-RETAIN-THE-DECAY-ACROSS-SCALES — Must retain scale-dependent decay before summing error bounds. Cannot count every scale at the largest bound and then regard the resulting linear loss as necessary: exact signed regrouping can reduce that cost to a harmonic sum. Must still balance the complete complementary tail after improving the finite-prefix estimate; an improved scale-counting loss does not itself remove the cutoff power or prove an absolute ceiling. The [full-source quotient and harmonic-depth estimate](../../3-formalized/edq-quotient-harmonic-tail-bound.md), including one unconditional bound uniform over all tail depths, is closed in Lean.

MUST-261 — [Lean for the attained case; paper for the rest] **The O-lane open core is a growth rate, not a sign: the pole-vanishing ground energy `μ₀` is negative at most like `−e^{B}` (trivial prime-side bound), and any attained excess `Θ` makes it negative at least like `−e^{2ΘB}/B³` (MUST-260). Hence `μ₀(B) ≥ −C e^{εB}` at every aperture forces every attained excess to be `≤ ε/2`** (`ConstrainedWeilPolarGroundDecay.two_mul_attainedExcess_le_of_polarGroundEnergy_ge`), and the O-lane target "prove `μ₀ ≥ 0` at every aperture" can be replaced by "prove `μ₀` is not exponentially negative at any positive rate" once the non-attained case is covered. Prime-side reading: on the pole-vanishing subspace `V₀` of `L²(−A, A)` the prime block of Suzuki's kernel form is the quadratic form of the prime translation operator `T = Σ_n Λ(n) n^{−½} τ_{log n}` (`(Tu)(x) = Σ Λ(n)n^{−½} u(x + log n)`), `Q(u) = A(2A)‖u‖² + ∫K₊(‖u‖² − g) − 2 Re⟨u, Tu⟩`, and the trivial bound `‖T‖ ≤ Σ_{n<e^{2A}} Λ(n)n^{−½} ~ 2e^{A}` gives the exponent `1`; the exponent `2Θ` is what the zeros force. Therefore **the source problem of the O lane is exactly the exponential growth rate of the pole-compressed prime translation operator `P_{V₀} T P_{V₀}` on `L²(−A, A)`: rate `< 1` by any margin is a zero-free strip, rate `→ 0` is RH.** The compression by the two exponential moments is what removes the prime-number-theorem main term `e^{A}` from the uncompressed operator (for the indicator of `[−A, A]` the uncompressed form is `≍ e^{A}`); no unconditional estimate of the compressed norm below rate `1` is known to the corpus, and the attempt of this cycle (bounding `|g(log n)| ≤ ‖u‖²` termwise) reproduces exactly the trivial rate. Non-attained case: the quantitative Bombieri–Yoshida ("`Φ_h(t) ≥ −Ce^{εt}` ⟹ every `|Im z_q| ≤ ε`") is provable by the corpus's Widder–Landau engine with the halving stopped at the real pole `ε` of `C/(s − ε)`; it is recorded as the open Lean item on the Bohr-mean conjecture card.

MUST-FIX-COMPENSATION-BY-FULL-TAIL-ADMISSIBILITY — Must determine compensating terms from the complete source and prove they preserve finite energy. Cannot freely tune a scalar on finite windows when an incorrect coefficient leaves a linear tail: under reciprocal-square weights, its squared-energy summands have a nonzero limit. Must distinguish the uniquely admissible correction at each fixed cutoff from control of its energy as the cutoff grows. The [exact source mass and finite-energy classification](../../3-formalized/edq-counterterm-rigidity.md) are closed in Lean; the needed cofinal growth ceiling remains open.

MUST-RETAIN-COMPENSATION-THROUGH-HISTORY — Must propagate compensating terms through every rescaling and earlier cutoff used in a recurrence. Cannot infer that the accumulated correction is negligible from smallness of its coefficient at the current cutoff: the complete history can produce a component with linear energy growth. Must retain that component in the complete signed recurrence. The [exact compensated renewal and positive linear forcing-energy bounds](../../3-formalized/edq-log-prime-forcing.md) are closed in Lean; the joint source ceiling remains open.

MUST-262 — [Lean] **Sub-exponential negativity of the pole-vanishing ground energy is RH: if for every `ε > 0` there is `C ≥ 0` with `μ₀(B) ≥ −C e^{εB}` at every aperture `B`, then the Riemann hypothesis holds; quantitatively, `μ₀(B) ≥ −C e^{εB}` for all `B` (one fixed `ε ≥ 0`, `C ≥ 0`) forces every Xi zero to satisfy `|Re ρ − ½| ≤ ε` (sharpened to `ε/2` in MUST-264).** `CCMRestrictedWeilPolarBombieriYoshidaQuantitative.riemannHypothesis_of_polarGroundEnergy_subexponential`, `abs_im_xiSpectralPoint_le_of_polarGroundEnergy_ge`. This replaces the O-lane endpoint "`μ₀ ≥ 0` at every aperture" (Bombieri–Yoshida, `riemannHypothesis_of_polarGroundEnergy_nonneg`) by a strictly weaker one: positivity is not required, only the absence of exponential blow-down at any positive rate; and it closes the non-attained case left open by MUST-259/260/CANNOT-331 without any window averaging. Mechanism (three modules, no RH input): (i) `WeightedScrewLandauEngineThreshold` — for a bounded real weighted screw divisor `Φ_W` with `Φ_W(t) ≥ −C₀e^{εt}` (`0 ≤ ε < ½`), the shifted divisor `Φ_W + C₀e^{εt}` is nonnegative, its Laplace transform is the atom series plus the single real pole `C₀/(s − ε)`, and the Widder–Landau halving descends the abscissa of exponential moments from `½` to `ε` along `ε + (½ − ε)/2ⁿ` (the pole-free ball radius is `min(r/2, (r − ε)/2)`); (ii) `WeightedScrewPoleContradictionThreshold` — identity theorem on the connected set `{Re s > ε} ∖ {±iγ_q}` and the pole contradiction at any spectral pole with `Re s₀ > ε`, giving `|Im z_q| ≤ ε` for every copy by the reflection symmetry; (iii) the transfer: the polar-translated two-bump sources have `L²` norm `≤ 200(1 + cosh ½)²(|t| + 4)` at aperture `|t| + 6`, so the ground-energy bound gives `Re Φ₁(t) ≥ −K(1 + t)e^{εt} ≥ −(K/δ)e^{(ε+δ)t}`, and the threshold engine caps the excess at `ε + δ` for every small `δ`. Consequences: (a) together with MUST-260, the exponential growth rate of `−μ₀` and the supremum of the excesses determine each other: rate `≥ 2Θ` when `Θ` is attained, and every zero's excess is at most the rate; the classical analogue is `ψ(x) − x = O(x^{½+ε}) ∀ε ⟺ RH`, now stated for Suzuki's constrained form in the aperture variable; (b) the prime-side reformulation of MUST-261 becomes exact: **RH ⟺ the pole-compressed prime translation operator `P_{V₀}(Σ_n Λ(n)n^{−½}τ_{log n})P_{V₀}` on `L²(−A, A)` has sub-exponential growth in `A`** (its trivial growth is `e^{A}`; growth rate `ε` gives the strip `|Re ρ − ½| ≤ ε` for every zero, and `2Θ ≤ ε` when the top excess `Θ` is attained); (c) the receiver status is unchanged: no source estimate below rate `1` is known.

MUST-263 — [Lean] **Sub-exponential one-sided control of the √-scale prime hinge sum is RH: for `t ≥ 0` let `H(t) = Σ_{2 ≤ n ≤ e^t} Λ(n) n^{−½} (t − log n)` and let `S(t)` be its explicit smooth part (`4e^{t/2} + 4e^{−t/2} − 8 + (linear) + (Lerch constant) − (Lerch tail)`). Then RH holds iff for every `ε > 0` there is `C_ε` with `H(t) ≤ S(t) + C_ε e^{εt}` for all `t ≥ 0`; quantitatively, `H(t) ≤ S(t) + C e^{εt}` for all `t ≥ 0` with one fixed `ε ≥ 0` forces every Xi zero into `|Re ρ − ½| ≤ ε`.** `SuzukiScrewSubexponentialCriterion.riemannHypothesis_iff_screwPrimeSum_subexponential`, `abs_im_xiSpectralPoint_le_of_screwPrimeSum_le`, and the same for Suzuki's screw function `Ψ = S − H` (`riemannHypothesis_iff_suzukiPsiSubexponentialBelow`, `abs_im_xiSpectralPoint_le_of_suzukiPsi_ge`). Mechanism: the threshold Widder–Landau engine of MUST-262 with the constant weight `W ≡ 1`, the realness of the Xi screw divisor, and Suzuki's prime–Lerch identity `Ψ = S − H` on the source half-line. Only an upper bound on the prime sum is needed (no lower bound), and only one-sided sub-exponential room; this is the Cesàro-smoothed, √-normalised, one-sided form of the classical Landau lemma `ψ(x) − x ≤ C x^{θ} ⟹ Θ ≤ θ`, now on a prime object with no pole term and no smoothing parameter. Receiver status: the trivial bound is `H(t) − S(t) = O(e^{t/2})`, i.e. `ε = ½`, and no unconditional `ε < ½` is known; lowering `ε` by any amount is exactly the zero-free-strip wall.

MUST-264 — [Lean] **Sharp rate dictionary for the constrained ground energy: `μ₀(B) ≥ −C e^{εB}` at every aperture forces every Xi zero into `|Re ρ − ½| ≤ ε/2`, so twice every excess is at most any exponential rate bounding `μ₀` from below; with MUST-260 (attained top excess `Θ` forces `μ₀(B) ≤ −c e^{2ΘB}/B³`) the exponential order of `−μ₀` is exactly `2Θ` whenever the supremal excess `Θ` is attained, and is at least `2Θ_sup` in general.** `CCMRestrictedWeilPolarBombieriYoshidaSharp.abs_im_xiSpectralPoint_le_half_of_polarGroundEnergy_ge`, `two_mul_abs_im_xiSpectralPoint_le_of_polarGroundEnergy_ge`. Mechanism: Suzuki's screw form is translation invariant on zero-mean tests (`suzukiScrewSmoothForm_translate`: each ray coefficient gains `e^{i z_q s}`, the reflected one `e^{−i z_q s}`), and the polar translate multiplier commutes with translation; centring the two-bump test `b_t − b_0` at `±t/2` keeps its form value but needs only the aperture `|t|/2 + 6` instead of `|t| + 6`, so the ground-energy bound transfers to `Re Φ_W(t) ≥ −K e^{(ε/2+δ)t}` and the threshold engine caps the excess at `ε/2`. This halves the cap of MUST-262 and is the correct exponent: the form is quadratic in the test, so a zero of excess `θ` produces growth `e^{2θ·(aperture)}`. Consequence: the non-attained shadow on the ground front is closed on the lower-bound side without window averaging — `lim sup (log(−μ₀(B)))/B ≥ 2Θ_sup` unconditionally under not-RH — and the only missing half of the exact identity `rate(−μ₀) = 2Θ_sup` is the zero-side upper bound `μ₀(B) ≥ −C_δ e^{(2Θ+δ)B}`, which needs a sampling inequality for compactly supported tests against the zero measure (not in the corpus). Receiver status unchanged: no source estimate below rate `1` is known.


MUST-PAY-GROWING-WITNESS-COSTS — Must control witness cutoffs, inverse margins, tolerances and preparation costs along the actual growing source history. Every fixed finite projection can have complete directional control, a finite witness family and a uniform local perturbation margin, while none of those statements bounds the cost as the projection changes. Cannot replace an exhaustive bounded-energy construction by separate existential witnesses in each finite dimension. Even actual batches of arbitrary finite length at arbitrarily late cutoffs, including bounded preparation and every batch prefix, may permanently omit retained coordinates and have a budget depending on the chosen field. Must prove compatibility, exhaustion and uniform costs in the same construction. The [finite-source control theorem](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-finite-retained-directional-control.md) and [actual arbitrary-field batches](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-arbitrary-retained-prime-batches.md) are closed in Lean; the cofinal cost law remains open.

MUST-TEST-SHARED-COEFFICIENTS-ACROSS-SCALES — Must test a shared cancelling coefficient against separated source scales before requiring that coefficient itself to be small. A fixed separation of magnitudes forces a positive total squared defect for every possible coefficient. Injective scale pairings can accumulate a linear prefix-square floor without any coefficient decay estimate. Must retain the actual energy weights when converting that floor into growth of the complete response. The [exact dilation separation and power-law current bound](../../3-formalized/edq-dilation-separated-power-growth.md) are closed in Lean and reduce a sufficient RH upper target to cofinal subpower growth; that upper bound remains unproved.

MUST-COMPARE-AT-THE-PRESERVED-SOURCE-SCALE — Must compare a growth upper bound with the scale on which the source's separating defect is preserved. An auxiliary support may be arbitrarily larger; a subpower bound in that support alone need not contradict a power lower bound in the smaller preserved scale. Must track uniformity in the auxiliary parameter before selecting it adaptively. The [exact cutoff-average current and uniform inner-scale power floor](../../3-formalized/edq-cutoff-averaging-transfer.md) are closed in Lean. Their adaptive subpower criterion still requires an independent full-source upper bound.

MUST-RECOMPUTE-COVARIANCE-AFTER-ACCUMULATION — Must account for correlations between different increments when estimating an accumulated response. A sum of increment squares is not the same quantity as a sum of squared cumulative sums. For the weight 1/(n(n+1)) through T, accumulation produces the exact pair kernel 1/max(j,k)-1/(T+1), including every unequal pair. Must apply compensation before squaring, track the cost of any bound transferring increment energy to cumulative energy, and retain the complementary tail for a full-source claim. Cannot assume that deleting distant pairs preserves positivity or gives an upper bound: must justify the truncation and control the signed omitted contribution. The [exact compensated arithmetic covariance and full-tail decomposition](../../3-formalized/edq-averaged-divisor-covariance.md) are closed in Lean; their representation alone provides no required growth ceiling.

MUST-RETAIN-ACTUAL-SOURCE-SUPPORT — Must distinguish coordinates that can occur in the actual source from all directions in an ambient coordinate space. A complete direction test can require a larger source aperture than the current state carries. Must prove that every omitted coordinate is exactly zero and that the complete observable is unchanged before restricting support. Must retain exact correlations among the remaining coordinates when their values come from one common source at different scales. Cannot infer an energy lower bound from a witness-aperture lower bound. Evidence: [exact support, initial sign chambers and common-source energy](../../3-formalized/ccm-fu-retained-support-and-common-head.md).

MUST-PRESERVE-CUTOFF-QUANTIFIERS — Must match a source construction to the cutoff quantifiers of its proved RH receiver. A bound required at every cutoff cannot be replaced by bounded values at selected cofinal endpoints without a proved propagation argument. A sufficient cofinal prefix construction must bound every earlier cutoff using that earlier cutoff's normalization and one common constant. Must distinguish exhaustion of source operations at selected endpoints from control of the whole cutoff sequence. Evidence: the literal every-cutoff `MobiusDivisorSquareRootAbelBounded` receiver and the [reserve-induction quantifier audit](../ccm-fu-reserve-extension-induction.md).


MUST-RETAIN-COMPLETION-POLYNOMIALS — Must retain the exact deterministic terms introduced by completing a truncated signed sum before demanding a smaller bound on its remainder. Cannot transfer cancellation of a complete sum to its complementary tail: the tail can contain a quadratic leading term while the complete sum is bounded by every power greater than one. Must prove uniformity as the original cutoff grows. The [complete coprime-column bound and exact quadratic-tail identity](../../3-formalized/edq-coprime-column-cancellation.md) are closed in Lean; they do not supply the required bound for the complete compensated energy. Must retain the leading coefficient and later tail when only the corrected initial portion is controlled by every positive power. The [unconditional subpower initial remainder](../../3-formalized/edq-coprime-head-subpower.md) establishes precisely that distinction.

MUST-AGGREGATE-CORRECTION-COSTS — Must bound the total absolute cost of corrections when transferring a growth estimate across a growing family of components. A bound for each fixed component does not control their growing sum. Cannot infer different power-growth behavior from a finite constant-budget failure when the total correction is already bounded by every positive cutoff power. The [unconditional aggregate remainder and total-variation comparison](../../3-formalized/edq-gcd-head-subpower.md) are closed in Lean. Must still control the retained leading terms and complete later tail.

CANNOT-335 — [paper, 2026-09-08; Lean for the constant-coefficient step: `GaloisForLFunctions.HTGeometry.interval_integral_normSq_expSum_ge`] **Bandwidth-`≤ 1` pair statistics cannot detect isolated off-line zeros at the mean-gap scale across different real parts.** Detecting a set of zeros whose ordinate gaps are `(1+Δ)` times the mean gap `2π/log T` needs a Montgomery–Vaughan average over a window of length `≥ 1/(1+Δ)` in the scale variable `α` (or `≍ log T/(1+Δ)` in the Laplace variable), and the off-line amplitude `T^{α(β−1/2)}` varies over such a window by `T^{δ/(1+Δ)}`; bottom-amplitude bounds close the argument only when all real parts lie in one band of width `O(1/log T)`, because cross terms between two bands compare the top amplitude of one with the bottom amplitude of the other. Consequence: any bandwidth-`≤ 1` density statement for off-line zeros is either at fixed ordinate gaps (`≫ 1/log T`, the spaced-set count of CANNOT-332) or inside a single real-part band; the mean-gap isolation law `#{ρ : Re ρ ≥ 1/2+δ, (1+Δ)-spaced} ≪ T^{1−2δΔ/(1+Δ)+o(1)}` remains open, and with the tapered-block evader it would make the mean gap the exact threshold between detectable and invisible violations of RH. Flattening the form by one band's amplitude closes the two-band case with explicit exponents, and in general detects exactly the bands that dominate the convex energy envelope `max_i N_i T^{2αδ_i}` over an interval of length `1/(1+Δ)`; ladder configurations (bands with geometrically decreasing counts whose energy crossovers are spaced below `1/(1+Δ)`) are spaced, multi-band, and invisible to every scale window. The averaged pair form resolves real parts only at scale (window length)·(distance from the line), never at `1/log T`.

CANNOT-336 — [Lean, 2026-09-08: `RHTower.eventually_card_singleBand_le`, explicit form `RHTower.eventually_card_singleBand_le_example`] **A violation of RH at density `T^{1−o(1)}` cannot be a single isolated band.** For large `T`, if every zero with `Re ρ > 1/2+δ₀` and `0 < γ ≤ T` has real part in one band `[σ, σ+(1/20)/log T]` with `σ ≥ 1/2+4δ₀`, and these zeros have pairwise ordinate gaps at least `50` mean gaps (`50·2π/log T`), then they number at most `(6e³/π) T^{1−(6/5)(σ−1/2)} log T` — unconditionally, from the uniform pair-correlation asymptotic and the Montgomery–Vaughan mean value over a window of scales. Consequently a dense failure of RH must either spread its off-line zeros across several real-part bands (the open ladder question, CANNOT-335), or pack them below the mean gap (tapered blocks, CANNOT-332), or keep other zeros far from the line.

CANNOT-337 — [Lean, 2026-09-08: `RHTower.eventually_card_dominantBand_le`] **Among several isolated bands of off-line zeros, a band that dominates the pair-form energy at both ends of a scale window cannot be dense.** For a family of disjoint real-part bands of width `τ/log T`, ordinates pairwise `D` mean gaps apart, all other zeros within `δ₀` of the line: if band `i₀` (at distance `≥ 4δ₀` from the line) dominates the family's Montgomery–Vaughan budgets at both ends of the window `[a, a+ℓ]` and carries at least half the amplitude mass at the top scale, then `N_{i₀} ≤ C · T^{1−2a(σ_{i₀}−1/2)} log T`, unconditionally. Together with CANNOT-336 (one band) and CANNOT-335 (why windows are needed), the only isolated configurations still compatible with a dense RH failure are ladders: families in which no band dominates over any window of length `1/D`. [Wide form, Lean 2026-09-08: `RHTower.eventually_card_dominantBand_le_wide` — the same law for bands of any width `τ/log T`, with the damped Montgomery–Vaughan constants `ℓ₀e^{−2τℓ₀} − 26/(πD) − 9τ/(π²D²)` (dominant band, lower) and `e^{2τ(a+ℓ)}(ℓ + 26/(πD) + 9τ/(π²D²))` (other bands, upper), no `τ ≤ 1` or `a+ℓ ≤ 1` hypothesis; a ladder must therefore keep every band narrower than its isolation allows.]

CANNOT-338 — [Lean, 2026-09-08: `GaloisForLFunctions.HTGeometry.interval_integral_normSq_mvBandSum_ge_damped`, `RHTower.eventually_card_singleBand_le_wide`, explicit form `RHTower.eventually_card_singleBand_le_wide_example`] **Isolated off-line zeros cannot hide in a real-part band narrower than their isolation.** The damped Montgomery–Vaughan inequality: for frequencies with pairwise gaps `≥ D` and exponents in `[θ_top − K, θ_top]` with `θ_top ≥ 0`, the mean square of `Σ_r d_r e^{θ_r α} e^{2πi u_r α}` over `[a, a+ℓ]` is at least `(ℓ₀ e^{−2Kℓ₀} − 26/(πD) − 9K/(π²D²)) Σ_r |d_r|² e^{2θ_r a}` for every `0 ≤ ℓ₀ ≤ ℓ`, uniformly in the number of terms, with no decay-versus-gap hypothesis (the pair integral `(e^{wb} − e^{wa})/w` with `w = (θ_r+θ_s) + 2πi(u_r−u_s)` differs from the pure Hilbert term by `≤ K/(π²(u_r−u_s)²)` because `|w| ≥ |Im w|`; the two endpoint Hilbert forms are Montgomery–Vaughan, the inverse-square remainder is the spacing lemma). Consequence: for large `T`, off-line zeros in one band `[σ, σ + τ/log T]` of any width `τ ≥ 5/3`, `σ ≥ 1/2 + 4δ₀`, pairwise `100τ` mean gaps apart, all other zeros within `δ₀` of the line, number at most `(3e³τ/π) T^{1−(6/5)(σ−1/2)} log T`. The admissible band width grows linearly with the isolation: the unit of real-part resolution of the averaged pair form is (isolation in mean gaps)/`log T`, replacing the `1/log T` of CANNOT-336. The damped inequality itself is the quantitative form of the Ingham inequality for complex frequencies with bounded real parts (Komornik–Loreti; Edward, JMAA 2006); the explicit polynomial constants and the band consequence are the new content.

CANNOT-339 — [Lean, 2026-09-08: `RHTower.re_suzukiScrewSmoothForm_ge_topExcess`, `RHTower.re_suzukiScrewSmoothForm_ge_half`] **The Weil form cannot be more negative than the top excess allows, in the `L¹` normalisation.** Let `Θ_sup = sup_ρ |Re ρ − 1/2|` over the zeros of `ξ` and `S₂ = Σ_ρ |ρ − 1/2|⁻²` (counted with multiplicity). For every smooth zero-mean test `φ` supported in `[−a, a]`, the screw form `Q(φ) = Σ_ρ c̄_ρ(φ) c_ρ(φ)/z_ρ²` (the Weil explicit-formula form of the primitive of `φ`, `z_ρ = −i(ρ − 1/2)`) satisfies `Re Q(φ) ≥ −(e^{Θ_sup |a|} + 1)² ‖φ‖₁² S₂`, unconditionally; with `Θ_sup ≤ 1/2` this is the trivial rate `e^{|a|}`. Together with the corpus's attained-top negativity (a zero at excess `Θ` gives tests of polynomial `L¹` norm with `Re Q ≤ −c e^{2Θa}`), the exponential growth rate of the `L¹`-normalised negative part of the Weil form is exactly `2Θ` when the top excess is attained: **`Θ_sup` is exactly half the exponential rate of Weil negativity per unit `‖φ‖₁²`** [Lean: `RHTower.screwFormL1Rate_iff`: for `0 ≤ ε < 1`, `Re Q(φ) ≥ −C e^{ε|a|}‖φ‖₁²` for all tests and apertures with one constant `C` iff `2Θ_sup ≤ ε`; the lower half is the centred two-bump / threshold Widder–Landau cap with the two-bump tests' `L¹` norm `≤ 10(1+cosh ½)`]. **RH ⟺ the `L¹`-normalised Weil negativity is sub-exponential in the aperture** [Lean: `RHTower.riemannHypothesis_iff_screwFormL1Rate_subexponential`]. The same identity in the `L²`-source normalisation (the constrained ground energy) has only its lower half; its upper half is a local zero-density statement, because the negative part of the form is a log-density sampling sum at the off-line zeros while the `L²` normalisation carries no logarithmic weight.
